**** SNOWMOBILES ****
§ 72.01 REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Snowmobiles may operate within the village limits of the village upon
an approved snowmobile route as marked by signs placed under the authority of
village officials.
   (B)   No snowmobile shall be operated in excess of 20 mph.
   (C)   Each person operating a snowmobile shall observe all traffic signs and
signals, and all of the rules and regulations applying to traffic within the
village.
(Prior Code, § 6-4-1) (Ord. 2004-2, passed 2-3-2004) Penalty, see §
72.99
Cross-reference:
   Specific areas with restrictions on operating a snowmobile, see
Chapter 73, Schedule II
**** NON-HIGHWAY VEHICLES, LOW-SPEED VEHICLES ****
§ 72.15 OPERATION.
   The use of non-highway and low-speed vehicles on the streets, roadways, and
alleys of the village shall be permissible subject to the provisions of this
subchapter.
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 72.16 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ALL-TERRAIN VEHICLE. Any motorized off-highway device designed to travel
primarily off-highway, 50 inches or less in width, having a manufacturer’s dry
weight of 1,500 pounds or less, traveling on four or more non-highway tires,
designed with a seat or saddle for operator use, and handlebars or steering
wheel for steering control, except equipment such as lawnmowers.
   GOLF CART. A vehicle specifically designed and intended for the purposes of
transporting one or more persons aud their golf clubs or maintenance equipment
while engaged in the playing of golf, supervising the play of golf, or
maintaining the condition of the grounds on a public or private golf course.
   LOW-SPEED VEHICLE. Any four-wheeled vehicle with a maximum speed greater
than 20 mph, but not greater than 25 mph, that conforms with the federal motor
vehicle safety standards set forth in 49 C.F.R. § 571.500.
   NEIGHBORHOOD VEHICLE. A self-propelled, electric-powered, four-wheeled motor
vehicle (or a self-propelled, gasoline-powered, four-wheeled motor vehicle with
an engine displacement under 1,200 cubic centimeters) that is capable of
attaining in one mile a speed of more than 20 mph, but not more than 25 mph,
and which does not conform to federal regulations under 49 C.F.R. § 571.500.
   NON-HIGHWAY VEHICLE. A motor vehicle not specifically designed to be used on
a public highway, including:
      (1)   An all-terrain vehicle;
      (2)   A golf cart;
      (3)   A neighborhood vehicle;
      (4)   An off-highway motorcycle; and
      (5)   A recreational off-highway vehicle.
   OFF-HIGHWAY MOTORCYCLE. Any motorized device designed to travel primarily
off-highway on two wheels, having a seat or saddle for the use of the operator,
upon or by which any person, persons or property may be transported or drawn.
   RECREATIONAL OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE. Any motorized off-highway device designed
to travel primarily off-highway, 64 inches or less in width, having a
manufacturer’s dry weight of 2,000 pounds or less, traveling on four or more
non-highway tires, designed with a non-straddle seat and a steering wheel for
steering control, except equipment such as lawnmowers.
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 72.17 REGULATIONS.
   (A)   (1)   No person shall operate a non-highway vehicle at any time from
November 1 of every year to April 30 of the following year.
      (2)   No non-highway vehicle may be operated at a speed limit in excess
of 15 mph.
      (3)   All non-highway vehicles shall be operated only on streets where
the posted speed limit is 35 mph or less. The foregoing provision shall not
prohibit a non-highway vehicle from crossing a road or street at an
intersection where the road or street has a posted speed limit of more than 35
mph.
      (4)   A person may not operate a non-highway vehicle upon any street,
highway, or roadway unless he or she:
         (a)   Is 21 years of age or older; and
         (b)   Has a valid driver’s license issued in his or her name by the
Secretary of State, or such other applicable foreign jurisdiction.
      (5)   No person operating a non-highway vehicle shall make a direct
crossing upon or across any tollroad, interstate highway, or controlled access
highway. No person operating a non-highway vehicle shall make a direct crossing
upon or across any other highway under the jurisdiction of the state except at
an intersection of the highway with another public street, road, or highway.
      (6)   No non-highway vehicle may be operated on a roadway unless, at a
minimum, it has the following: brakes, a steering apparatus, tires, a rearview
mirror, red reflectorized warning devices in the front and rear, a slow moving
emblem (as required of other vehicles in § 12-709 of the State Vehicle Code,
being 625 ILCS 5/12-709) on the rear of the non-highway vehicle, a headlight
that emits a white light visible from a distance of 500 feet to the front, a
tail lamp that emits a red light visible from at least 100 feet from the rear,
brake lights, and turn signals. When operated on a roadway, a non-highway
vehicle shall have its headlight and tail lamps lighted as required by § 12-201
of the State Vehicle Code, being 625 ILCS 5/12-201.
      (7)   A person who drives or is in actual physical control of a non-
highway vehicle on a roadway while under the influence is subject to §§ 11-500
through 11-502 of the State Vehicle Code, being 625 ILCS 5/11-500 through 5/11-
502, and all other applicable laws.
      (8)   The operation of a non-highway vehicle upon any street, roadway, or
alley is subject to the provisions of Chapter 11 of the State Vehicle Code,
being 625 ILCS 5/11, concerning the rules of the road and all applicable
village ordinances, as the same may be amended from time to time.
      (9)   No person shall be permitted to operate an off-highway motorcycle
upon any street, roadway, or alley in the village unless said off-highway
motorcycle meets all of the requirements of the State Vehicle Code and is
licensed to travel on streets thereunder.
      (10)   No non-highway vehicle shall be operated on any village sidewalks
or other surfaces other than village streets.
      (11)   Non-highway vehicles shall be permitted to operate at only the
following times: on Mondays through Thursdays, from 6:00 a.m. until 11:00 p.m.;
on Fridays and Saturdays, from 6:00 a.m. until 12:00 a.m.; and on Sundays, from
6:00 a.m. until 11:00 p.m.
   (B)   (1) No person shall operate a low-speed vehicle at any time from
November 1 of every year to April 30 of the following year.
      (2)   No low-speed vehicle may be operated at a speed limit in excess of
15 mph.
      (3)   All low-speed vehicles shall be operated only on streets were the
posted speed limit is 35 mph or less. The foregoing provision shall not
prohibit a low-speed vehicle from crossing a road or street at an intersection
where the road or street has a posted speed limit of more than 35 mph, subject
to the conditions set forth in division (B)(4) below.
      (4)   Low-speed vehicles may cross a street at an intersection where the
street being crossed has a posted speed limit of not more than 45 mph. Low-
speed vehicles may not cross a street with a speed limit in excess of 45 mph
unless the crossing is at an intersection controlled by a traffic light or
four-way stop sign.
      (5)   (a)   No low-speed vehicle may be operated on any street unless, at
a minimum, it has the following: a parking brake, a steeling apparatus, tires,
a windshield that conforms to the federal safety standards on glazing materials
as set forth in 49 C.F.R. § 571.205, a vehicle identification number, seat
belts, a rearview mirror, an exterior rearview mirror mounted on the driver’s
side of the vehicle, red reflectorized warning devices on each rear side and
one on the center rear of the vehicle, a headlight that emits a white light
visible from a distance of 500 feet to the front, a tail lamp that emits a red
light visible from at least 100 feet from the rear, brake lights, and front and
rear turn signals.
         (b)   When operated on a street, a low-speed vehicle shall have its
headlight and tail lamps lighted as required by § 12-201 of the State Vehicle
Code, being 625 ILCS 5/12-201.
      (6)   A person may not operate a low-speed vehicle upon any street,
highway, or roadway unless he or she:
         (a)   Is 21 years of age or older; and
         (b)   Has a valid driver’s license issued in his or her name by the
Secretary of State, or such other applicable foreign jurisdiction.
      (7)   The operation of a low-speed vehicle upon any street, roadway, or
alley is subject to the provisions of Chapter 11 of the State Vehicle Code,
being 625 ILCS 5/11, concerning the rules of the road and all applicable
village ordinances, as the same may be amended from time to time.
      (8)   A person who drives or is in actual physical control of a low-speed
vehicle on a roadway while under the influence is subject to §§ 11-500 through
11-502 of the State Vehicle Code, being 625 ILCS 5/11-500 through 5/11-502, and
all other applicable laws.
      (9)   No low-speed vehicle shall be operated on any village sidewalks or
other surfaces other than village streets.
      (10)   Low-speed vehicles shall be permitted to operate at only the
following times: on Mondays through Thursdays, from 6:00 a.m. until 11:00 p.m.;
on Fridays and Saturdays, from 6:00 a.m. until 12:00 a.m.; and on Sundays, from
6:00 a.m. until 11:00 p.m.
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 72.18 INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS.
   Any person who operates a non-highway or low-speed vehicle on a street,
highway, or roadway of the village shall be subject to the mandatory insurance
requirements under Article VI of Chapter 7 of the State Vehicle Code, being 625
ILCS 5/7.
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 72.19 SEATBELTS REQUIRED.
   During the operation of a non-highway or low-speed vehicle, the operator and
all passengers shall wear a seatbelt.
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 72.20 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   (A)   No person shall operate a non-highway or low-speed vehicle on the
village’s streets, roadways, or alleys without first obtaining an annual permit
from the village as provided herein. The annual cost of the permit shall be
$25. A permit, regardless of the date of its issuance, shall be valid, unless
suspended or revoked, from May 1 until April 30 of the following year. The cost
of a permit granted after May 1 shall be prorated on a per diem basis. Every
non-highway or low-speed vehicle shall be required to have a permit, and such
permit shall be non-transferable.
   (B)   Every application for a permit shall be made on a form supplied by the
village and shall contain the following information:
      (1)   Name and address of the applicant;
      (2)   Name of insurance carrier and policy number and/or a photocopy of
applicable liability insurance coverage card issued specifically for the non-
highway or low-speed to be operated pursuant to the permit;
      (3)   The serial number, make, model, and description of the non-highway
or low-speed vehicle; and
      (4)   Such other information as the village may require, including a
release and waiver of liability signed by applicant releasing the village and
its employees, and agreeing to indemnify and hold the village harmless,
including the costs of defense, from any and all claims, penalties, costs,
judgments, settlements, or causes of action resulting or arising from the
operation of applicant’s non-highway or low-speed vehicle on village streets.
   (C)   No permit shall be granted by the village until the non-highway or
low-speed vehicle shall be submitted to and pass an inspection by the village
(or its designee) to ensure compliance with the provisions of this chapter and
the State Vehicle Code.
   (D)   Each person operating a non-highway vehicle or low speed vehicle shall
carry the permit issued by the village at all times during the operation of
said vehicle
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   The village may suspend or revoke a permit granted hereunder
upon a finding that the holder thereof has violated any provision of §§
72.15 through
72.20, or there is a finding that the permittee cannot safely operate a non-
highway or low-speed vehicle on village streets.
      (2)   A violation of any provision of §§
72.15 through
72.20 shall be punishable by payment of a fine of not less than $50 and not
more than $500, and the village’s enforcement costs incurred, including its
attorney fees, for each offense. Any second or subsequent offense shall result
in the revocation of the village permit for a period of not less than one, nor
more than three years. To the extent that any violation of §§
72.15 through
72.20 may also constitute a violation of a minimal statute of the state, the
violator shall also be subject to criminal prosecution and all other penalties
as permitted by law
(Ord. 2018-2, passed 4-9-2018)
CHAPTER 73: TRAFFIC SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   Bicycles, skateboards, roller skates, in-line skates on right-of-way
      
II.   Snowmobiles on right-of-way
SCHEDULE I. BICYCLES, SKATEBOARDS, ROLLER SKATES, IN-LINE SKATES ON RIGHT-OF-
WAY.
   (A)   No bicycles, skateboards, roller skates, or in-line skates, or like
items, shall be operated, ridden, or used in any fashion on the sidewalks of
Main Street located between First Street and Second Street, and none shall be
operated, ridden, or used in any fashion on the sidewalks of Second Street
between Main Street and Blaine Street in the village. No bicycles, skateboards,
roller skates, or in-line skates, or like items shall be operated, ridden, or
used in any fashion in any part of the village so as to be a hazard to, or
interfere with, traffic or pedestrians.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of division (A) above shall be
fined not less than $10 nor more than $100 for each offense; however, the
notice of ordinance violation may contain a provision that any person violating
any provision of said division (A) above may avoid a court appearance and a
fine of not less than $10 and not more than $100 by paying the sum of $10 to
the Village Clerk’s office within 72 hours after issuance of the notice of said
ordinance violation. The village shall impound any equipment used in violation
of this schedule until such time as the fine is paid or for a period not to
exceed 30 days.
(Prior Code, § 6-3-1) (Ord. 99-1, passed 1-5-1999)
SCHEDULE II. SNOWMOBILES ON RIGHT-OF-WAY.
   Snowmobilers will be allowed to be used all streets and alleys, except Main
Street and State Route 72, within the village limits to reach the approved
snowmobile route within the village.
(Prior Code, § 6-4-1) (Ord. 2004-2, passed 2-3-2004)
CHAPTER 74: PARKING SCHEDULES
Schedule
      
I.   Parking encroachments prohibited
SCHEDULE I. PARKING ENCROACHMENTS PROHIBITED.
   Automobiles, trucks, and other vehicles shall be prohibited from parking
along either side of Third Street, marked State Route 72, within the corporate
limits of the village.
(Prior Code, § 6-2-8)

90.99   Penalty
**** NUISANCES ****
§ 90.01 NUISANCES DECLARED UNLAWFUL.
   It shall be unlawful and a violation of this subchapter for any person to
wilfully or negligently create, erect, maintain, or permit a nuisance to exist
anywhere within the village.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-1) Penalty, see §
90.99
Statutory reference:
   Power of village to define, prevent, and abate nuisances, see 65 ILCS 5/11-
60-2
§ 90.02 NUISANCES ENUMERATED.
   (A)   Any thing, act, omission, occupation, or condition which shall exist
or continue for such a length of time as to do any of the following is hereby
declared a nuisance:
      (1)   Substantially annoy, injure, or endanger the health, safety, peace,
or welfare of the public;
      (2)   In any way render the public insecure in life or property;
      (3)   Greatly offend the public morals or decency; or
      (4)   Unlawfully and substantially interfere with, obstruct, or render
dangerous for passage any street, sidewalk, alley, navigable body, or other
public way or place.
   (B)   Nuisances enumerated in this subchapter shall not be deemed exclusive,
but are in addition to, and shall be construed in conjunction with, any other
nuisances enumerated in statutes or other provisions of this code or ordinances
of the village.
   (C)   Any nuisance declared by statute, this subchapter, or other provisions
of this code or ordinances of the village shall be enforceable under the
provisions of this subchapter as if enumerated specifically herein.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-2)
§ 90.03 INVESTIGATION BY VILLAGE; POWER OF ENTRY.
   (A)   For the purpose of discovering nuisances, it shall be the duty of the
police, fire, health, and building officers and inspectors to investigate,
confirm, and report any nuisances coming to their attention.
   (B)   In order to investigate, confirm, and report nuisances, said officers
and inspectors shall, upon proper identification, be permitted at any
reasonable hour and in a reasonable manner, to visit and enter into or upon any
premises or property within the village to make an examination thereof, if they
shall have reasonable grounds to believe that a nuisance exists.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-3)
§ 90.04 ABATEMENT PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Nuisance determined. Whenever any nuisance shall be reported to or
investigated and determined by the police, fire, health, or building officers
or inspectors, notice shall be given to the owner or person in control of the
property or premises involved and to the appropriate village officials whose
functions include the abatement of such nuisances.
   (B)   Notice to abate.
      (1)   Whenever any health or building official or inspector, or any
police or fire officer, determines that a nuisance exists on any private
property or premises, the proper village official shall cause a written notice
to be served upon the owner or person in control of the property, or premises
who is causing, permitting, or maintaining such nuisance, which notice shall
give the person served ten days from the date of service of the notice to abate
the nuisance. Such notice shall be by means of personal service of a copy of
the complaint or by certified or registered mail of a copy of the complaint.
Personal service shall be by any authorized inspector from the village or by
any police officer of the Police Department, or any person authorized by law to
make personal service.
      (2)   Such notice shall fairly apprise such person of the nature of the
nuisance, his or her duty to abate or remove the nuisance within the time
provided therein, the penalty for failure to abate the same, and shall state
that, if said nuisance is abated by the village, liability for necessary
expenses to be incurred shall accrue as provided for in §
90.05.
   (C)   Time limits. It is hereby declared the duty of any person determined
to have created, caused, erected, maintained, or permitted a nuisance to exist
within the village to discontinue and abate such nuisance within ten days, or
such other time period as may be specified in the notice, from the time he or
she receives written notice thereof.
   (D)   Abatement by village. Whenever any nuisance is not abated by the
owner, lessee, or person in control of the premises or property affected within
the time provided by notice, the proper village officer shall cause the
abatement or removal of such nuisance.
   (E)   Summary abatement by village. Whenever any following nuisance occurs,
the proper village officer shall cause such nuisance to be summarily and
immediately abated and removed, regardless of any ten-day or other time period
specified by notice to the person responsible therefor; provided, however, that
in division (E)(1) below, such officer shall have first applied for and
obtained the written permission of the President for such summary abatement:
      (1)   Constitutes or is deemed to be an imminent or immediate danger to
the public health or safety; or
      (2)   Exists on public property.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-4)
§ 90.05 COSTS ASSESSED; LIEN.
   (A)   (1)   Any costs or expenses of abatement reasonably incurred by the
village pursuant to the provisions of §
90.04(D) shall be deemed a debt to the village by the owner, lessee, or person
in control of premises upon which such nuisance existed.
      (2)   Provided, that where specifically provided in this code or by
statute, such costs and expenses shall become a lien upon the real estate
affected, superior to all other liens and encumbrances, except tax liens.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the Village Attorney to enforce by civil
action any and all such debts or liens as provided in division (A) above.
(Prior Code, § 4-2-5)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 65 ILCS 5/11-20-7, 5/11-20-8, 5/11-20-12, and 5/11-
31-1
§ 90.06 HEALTH OFFICER APPOINTED.
   The Village President shall appoint a Health Officer, with the consent of
the Village Board.
**** PLANTS AND WEEDS ****
§ 90.20 WEEDS DEFINED.
   WEEDS, as used in this subchapter, shall include the following:
   (A)   NOXIOUS WEEDS, such as ragweed, giant and common; Canada thistles and
all of its varieties; perennial sow thistle; European bind weed; hoary cress;
leafy spurge; and Russian knapweed; and
   (B)   OTHER WEEDS, such as Burdock, cocklebur, jimson, blue vervain, common
milk weed, wild carrot, poison ivy, wild mustard, rough pigweed, lambsquarter,
wild lettuce, curled dock, smart weeds (all varieties), poison hemlock, wild
hemp, ox eye daisy, goldenrod, yellow hemlock, buckhorn, or other weeds of a
like kind.
(Prior Code, § 4-3-1)
§ 90.21 HEIGHT RESTRICTIONS.
   It shall be a nuisance and, therefore, unlawful for any owner, lessee, or
occupant, or any agent, servant, representative, or employee of any such owner,
lessee, or occupant, having charge or control of any lot within the village to
permit or maintain on any such lot, including the area located between the
property line and the curb or edge of roadway, any growth of weeds as herein
defined over eight inches in height from the ground. OWNER shall include the
legal title holder and/or the beneficial owner of any such lot.
(Prior Code, § 4-3-2)
§ 90.22 NUISANCE DECLARED; CUTTING, REMOVAL OF NEGLECTED WEEDS AND GRASS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any resident, occupant, owner, or agent of
any premises to allow any weed or lawn grass to grow a height exceeding seven
inches anywhere within the village. Any such weed or lawn grass exceeding such
height is hereby declared to be a public nuisance. It shall be the duty of
every owner or occupant of any lot or parcel within the village to remove said
weed or lawn grass.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of every resident, occupant, owner, or agent of
any premises within the village to remove all Canada thistles, ragweed, or
other noxious weeds, and it shall be unlawful for anyone to permit such weeds
to grow or remain on any lot or parcel in the village, and the same are hereby
declared to be a public nuisance.
   (C)   Notice in writing of a violation of divisions (A) or (B) above shall
be given by the Village Clerk to the owner, or his or her agent, and the
occupant of any lot or parcel in the village violating divisions (A) or (B)
above. Upon failure of the owner, or his or her agent, or the occupant to cut
and remove the offending weeds or grass as defined in divisions (A) or (B)
above within ten days of the mailing of notice, it shall be the duty of the
Village Clerk to arrange to have the offending weeds or grass cut or removed.
   (D)   The expense incurred by the village in cutting or removing said weeds
and/or grass, which expense shall include an administrative fee of $100, shall
be charged to and paid by the owner of the lot or parcel and shall be collected
by the village by suit or otherwise and shall be in addition to any applicable
fines or penalty.
**** ABANDONED, INOPERABLE VEHICLES AND TRAILERS ****
§ 90.35 ABANDONED VEHICLES.
   (A)   Abandonment of vehicles prohibited.
      (1)   The abandonment of a motor vehicle or other vehicle or any part
thereof on any highway, street, alley, public parking lot, park or any other
public area in the village is unlawful and any such offender shall be subject
to penalty as set forth in §
10.99.
      (2)   The abandonment of a motor vehicle or other vehicle or any part
thereof on private or public property, other than a highway, in view of the
general public, anywhere in the village is unlawful except on property of the
owner or bailee of such abandoned vehicle. A motor vehicle or other vehicle or
any part thereof so abandoned on private property may be authorized for removal
by the Village Police Department after a waiting period of seven days or more
following notification of the owner. A violation of this section is subject to
penalties as set forth under §
10.99.
   (B)   Notification to enforcement agencies. When an abandoned, lost, stolen
or unclaimed motor vehicle or other vehicle comes into the temporary possession
or custody of a person in the village, not the owner of the vehicle, such
person shall immediately notify the Village Police Department when the vehicle
is within the corporate limits of the village. Upon receipt of such
notification, the Police Department may authorize a towing service to remove
and take possession of the abandoned, lost, stolen or unclaimed motor vehicle
or other vehicle. The towing service will safely keep the towed vehicle and its
contents and maintain a record of the tow as set forth herein for the Village
Police Department until the vehicle is claimed by the owner or any other person
legally entitled to possession thereof, or until it is disposed of as provided
in this section.
   (C)   Removal of abandoned vehicles.
      (1)   When a motor vehicle or other vehicle is abandoned on a state
highway in the village ten hours or more, its removal by a towing service may
be authorized by the Village Police Department.
      (2)   When a motor vehicle or other vehicle is abandoned or left
unattended on a highway other than a toll highway, interstate highway or
expressway for 24 hours or more, its removal by a towing service may be
authorized by the Village Police Department.
      (3)   When an abandoned, unattended, wrecked, burned or partially
dismantled motor vehicle or other vehicle is creating a traffic hazard because
of its position in relation to the highway, street, alley, public parking lot,
park or any other public area, or its physical presence is causing the impeding
of traffic, its immediate removal from the highway, street, alley, public
parking lot, park or any other public area, or private property adjacent to the
highway by a towing service may be authorized by the Village Police Department.
      (4)   When a vehicle removal from either public or private property is
authorized by the Village Police Department, the owner of the vehicle will be
responsible for all towing costs.
§ 90.36 INOPERABLE VEHICLES AND TRAILERS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      INOPERABLE MOTOR VEHICLE. Any motor vehicle from which, for a period of
at least ten days, the engine, wheels or other parts have been removed, or on
which the engine, wheels or other parts have been altered, damaged or otherwise
so treated that the vehicle is incapable of being driven under its own power.
The term INOPERABLE MOTOR VEHICLE shall not include a motor vehicle which has
been rendered temporarily, or for a period not exceeding ten days, incapable of
being driven under its own power in order to perform ordinary service or repair
operations.
      INOPERABLE TRAILER. Any trailer from which, for a period of at least ten
days, the wheels or other parts have been removed, or on which wheels or other
parts have been altered, damaged or otherwise so treated that the trailer is
incapable of being pulled under the power of an operational vehicle.
      STREET OPERABLE.
         (a)   When referring to a motor vehicle, any motor vehicle which may
be legally operated on the roadways, streets, or highways of this state.
Examples of motor vehicles which are not STREET OPERABLE include ATVs, race
cars, stock cars, and any motor vehicle which, because of equipment or the lack
thereof, precludes the operation of that motor vehicle upon the roadways,
streets, and highways of this state.
         (b)   The term STREET OPERABLE as used in this section, and when
referring to a trailer shall mean any trailer which may be legally operated on
the roadways, streets, or highways of this state.
   (B)   Public nuisance declared.
      (1)   It is hereby declared that all inoperable motor vehicles, and motor
vehicles which are not street operable, whether on public or private property,
shall be stored in a garage.
      (2)   Any motor vehicle or part thereof which is inoperable or
deteriorated or in need of repair, or which is not street operable, which is
located for at least ten days upon public or private property within the
village, outside a garage is hereby declared a public nuisance.
   (C)   Repairs/time limit.
      (1)   Any inoperable motor vehicle which has been rendered temporarily,
for a period of at least ten days, incapable of being driven under its own
power in order to perform ordinary service or repair operations is hereby
declared to be a public nuisance. Said inoperative motor vehicle which has been
rendered temporarily, for a period of at least ten days, incapable of being
driven under its own power in order to perform ordinary service or repair
operations shall be placed in a garage.
      (2)   Any inoperable trailer which has been rendered temporarily, for a
period of at least ten days, incapable of being pulled under the power of an
operable vehicle in order to perform ordinary service or repair operations is
hereby declared to be a public nuisance. Said inoperative trailer which has
been rendered temporarily, for a period of at least ten days, incapable of
being pulled under the power of an operable vehicle in order to perform
ordinary service or repair operations shall be placed in an enclosed garage.
   (D)   Exceptions. Nothing in this section shall apply to any motor vehicle
that is kept in a building or garage so as to be completely out of view of the
general public, or a motor vehicle on the lawfully zoned premises of a place of
business engaged in repair, wrecking or junking of motor vehicles, provided
they are kept within an enclosure on all sides so as not to be in view of the
general public.
   (E)   Public nuisance declared. It shall be unlawful and it is hereby
declared to be a public nuisance to have a motor vehicle on property owned,
leased or controlled by any person that does not display a current license
plate or sticker issued by any state of the United States to the particular
motor vehicle on which it is displayed, whether or not said motor vehicle is
operable or inoperable; except that this chapter shall not apply to:
      (1)   Any motor vehicle is kept within a building or garage; or
      (2)   New or used car lots which are properly zoned for the same.
**** HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES AND CONDITIONS ****
§ 90.50 HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES AND MATERIALS PROHIBITED.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      HAZARDOUS MATERIALS. Any explosives and any radioactive material.
      HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE. Toxic, corrosive, an irritant, a strong sensitizer,
flammable, or which generates pressure through decomposition, heat, or other
means, if such substance or mixture of substances may cause substantial
personal injury or substantial illness during or as a proximate result of any
customary, or reasonably foreseeable handling or use.
      RADIOACTIVE MATERIAL. Any material or combination of materials which
spontaneously emits ionizing radiation.
(Prior Code, § 4-7-1)
   (B)   No person shall store or dispose of any hazardous substance or
hazardous material anywhere within the village.
(Prior Code, § 4-7-2)
(Ord. passed 10-10-1992) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.51 CONTAGIOUS DISEASES.
   (A)   Report required. Every physician called in to care for or treat a
person afflicted with a contagious disease or any epidemic disease shall make a
report of the same to the Health Officer within 24 hours after being called in.
In case no physician is in attendance, it shall be the duty of the person in
charge of or having the care of such person to make a report within 24 hours
from the time the disease is recognized.
   (B)   Quarantine.
      (1)   Health Officer’s duties. The Health Officer shall have charge of
the enforcement of the quarantine rules. He or she shall have the power and the
authority to place any premises, within which a contagious or epidemic disease
occurs, under quarantine, and the Health Officer shall determine the time when
the quarantine ends.
      (2)   Fumigation. Premises which have been quarantined in accordance with
the terms of division (B)(1) above shall be thoroughly fumigated or otherwise
freed from all risk of contagious disease, under the supervision of the Health
Officer before the quarantine shall end.
      (3)   Deliveries to premises. No person engaged in the delivery of food
or drink intended for human consumption shall enter any premises which are
quarantined because of the existence of a contagious or epidemic disease. No
containers or bottles shall be removed from any such premises until the
termination of quarantine, and no such container which has been left at such
premises during the quarantine shall be placed in use for carrying food or
drink until it has been thoroughly sterilized.
   (C)   Spreading unlawful. It is unlawful for any person to spread, willfully
or carelessly, any contagious disease or to so cause the spread of the same.
§ 90.52 OFFENSIVE PREMISES.
   (A)   No owner or occupant of any premises in the village shall suffer any
cellar, vault, private drain, pool, privy, sewer, stable, barnyard, pen or
place where any domestic animals or fowls are kept upon such premises to become
nauseous, foul, offensive or injurious to the public health or to his or her
neighbors.
   (B)   (1)   No person shall deposit, throw, discharge, place or leave the
dead body of an animal, or any nauseous, foul, offensive or putrid liquid or
any other substance or liquid whatsoever or substance likely to become
nauseous, foul, offensive or putrid within the village or within one-half mile
thereof, so as to be or become offensive or injurious to the health or comfort
of any persons residing within the village.
      (2)   No person shall, in removing any dead animal, excrement or other
nauseous, offensive or putrid liquid or substance, purposely or necessarily
cause the same to be offensive or annoying to any person.
   (C)   No person shall, within the village, steam, boil or render any tainted
lard or tallow, offal or other animal substance so as to taint the air and
render it unwholesome or offensive.
   (D)   (1)   Definitions. For the purpose of this division (D), the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
         FOOD. Any food or beverage intended for human consumption.
         FOOD DEALER. Means and includes every person, firm or corporation
engaged in conducting or operating any of the following businesses:
            1.   Fruit store or establishment for the retail sale of fresh
fruit and of berries;
            2.   Grocery store, or establishment for the sale at retail of
butter, cheese, vegetables or other provisions;
            3.   Ice cream parlor;
            4.   Meat market or establishment for the retail sale of fresh
meat, poultry or fish;
            5.   Retail beverage store;
            6.   Bakery; and/or
            7.   Restaurant.
      (2)   Sale of unwholesome food prohibited.
         (a)   No person shall sell, offer for sale or keep for the purpose of
selling or offering for sale any food of any kind which is spoiled or tainted
or is unwholesome and unfit for human consumption for any reason.
         (b)   All tainted or unwholesome food intended for human consumption
may be condemned by the Board of Health, and shall, thereupon, be seized and
destroyed by the Board of Health, the Health Officer or any police officer of
the village.
      (3)   Sale of adulterated food unlawful. It is unlawful to sell, offer
for sale, or keep for such purpose, any food or drink intended for human
consumption which has been adulterated by any material harmful in any way, or
which does not comply with the state statutes governing the same.
      (4)   Premises, sanitary regulations. Premises used for the sale or
storage of food intended for human consumption must be kept in a clean and
sanitary condition. It is unlawful to permit any accumulation of refuse, or
waste of any kind thereon for more than 24 hours; and it is unlawful to permit
any decaying animal or plant matter to remain on any such premises at all.
      (5)   Premises, kept free from flies and vermin. Premises used for the
storage of food intended for human consumption shall be kept free from flies
and vermin of all kinds.
      (6)   Employee regulations. All persons engaged in handling or coming in
contact with food or drink intended for human consumption shall keep themselves
clean and shall keep their clothes clean. It is unlawful to permit any person
afflicted with a contagious or venereal disease to handle any food intended for
sale for human consumption, or for any such person to handle any such food.
      (7)   Inspections of establishments. The Health Officer shall inspect, or
cause to be inspected, every place, building or establishment used for the
storage, handling, sale or preparation of food or drink intended for human
consumption. It shall be the duty of every food dealer to give to the Health
Officer such samples of each such food or drink as may be necessary for the
purpose of making an analysis of the same to determine whether it is clean and
wholesome.
      (8)   Utensils. Utensils for personal use in all places serving food or
drink to the public shall be thoroughly cleaned and sterilized after each such
usage.
§ 90.53 GARBAGE, REFUSE AND HARMFUL ATTRACTIONS.
   (A)   Receptacle required.
      (1)   It shall be the duty of every owner or his agent or occupant of any
house, building, flat or apartment, or tenement in the village where people
reside, board or lodge, or where animal or vegetable matter is prepared or
served, and at all times to maintain in good order and repair, a receptacle for
garbage, bottles, ashes and similar refuse.
      (2)   The receptacle for garbage shall be water-tight.
   (B)   Placement in street prohibited. No garbage or refuse of any kind shall
be deposited in any street, alley or public way, excepting as is provided in
this section; and no such refuse shall be so placed that it can be blown about
or scattered by the wind.
   (C)   Storage of refuse dangerous to health prohibited. It shall be unlawful
for any person owning, leasing, occupying or controlling any plot of ground to
store, dump or permit the accumulation of debris, refuse, garbage, inoperable
air conditioners, snowmobiles, motorcycles, bicycles, lawn mowers, appliances
and old furniture. Also tires, buckets, cans, wheelbarrows, or other containers
in a manner that may harbor mosquitoes, flies, insects, rodents, and other
animal pests that are offensive, injurious or dangerous to the health of
individuals or the public.
   (D)   Harmful conditions attractive to children prohibited. It shall be
unlawful for any person owning, leasing or controlling any plot of ground to
allow or have on said premises any condition to exist on said premises of such
a character as to be an attraction to children or other individuals, including,
but not limited to, mounds of dirt, piles of lumber, vehicles, or pools of
water which would cause children or other individuals to be attracted to the
same and thereby exposing children or other individuals to a dangerous
condition which would affect the public safety and welfare of children or other
individuals.
   (E)   Placement on private property, notice to remove, refusal. In the event
that garbage and debris is located upon private property of any owner of the
same within the village limits, the Village Clerk is directed to send a notice
in writing to the owner of such property giving the owner at least ten days to
remove the garbage and debris. In the event that the owner refuses or neglects
to remove such garbage and debris after such notice, the village may remove the
garbage and debris from such private property and may collect from the owner of
such private property a reasonable cost of the removal thereof. The costs are
to be a lien upon the real estate affected, superior to all subsequent liens
and encumbrances, except tax liens if within 60 days after such cost and
expense is incurred by the village, or the person performing the service by
authority of the village, in his or its own name, files notice of lien in the
office of the Recorder of Deeds of Ogle County. The notice shall consist of a
sworn statement setting out:
      (1)   A description of the real estate sufficient for identification
thereof;
      (2)   The amount of money representing the cost and expenses incurred or
payable for the service; and
      (3)   The date or dates when such cost and expense was incurred by the
village.
   (F)   Failure to remove garbage, lien.
      (1)   The lien set forth in division (E) above shall not be valid as to
any purchaser whose rights in and to such real estate have arisen subsequent to
removal of the garbage and debris and prior to the filing of such notice, and
the lien of the village shall not be valid as to any mortgagee, judgment
creditor or other lienor whose rights in and to such real estate arise prior to
the filing of such notice.
      (2)   Upon payment of the cost and expense by the owner of or persons
interested in such property after notice of lien has been filed, the lien shall
be released by the village, or person in whose name the lien has been filed,
and the release may be filed of record in the office of the Recorder of Deeds
of Ogle County.
      (3)   The lien may be in force by proceedings to foreclose as in case of
mortgages or mechanic’s lien. Suit to foreclose this lien shall be commenced
within two years after the date of filing the notice of lien.
§ 90.54 WATER.
   (A)   Water pollution prohibited. It is unlawful and a nuisance for any
person to obstruct or pollute any watercourse or source of water supply in the
village.
   (B)   Stagnant pools, deemed nuisance. Any stagnant pool of water in the
village is a nuisance. It is unlawful for any person to permit any such
nuisance to remain or exist on any property under his or her control.
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   (1)   Whenever in §§
90.01 through
90.05 any act is prohibited or is made, or declared to be unlawful or a
nuisance, or whenever the doing of any act is required or the failure to do any
act is declared to be unlawful, when no specific penalty is provided therefor,
any person violating such provision shall, upon conviction thereof, be subject
to a penalty as provided in §
10.99(A).
      (2)   (a)   It shall be unlawful and a violation of §§
90.01 through
90.05 for any person to neglect, refuse, or otherwise fail to remove or abate
any nuisance after expiration of the ten days or other specified time period
provided by notice thereof, and each 24 hours or fraction thereof during which
such nuisance continues or exists shall be deemed a separate offense.
         (b)   Any person who shall cause, permit, or allow a nuisance to
continue or exist as provided for in division (A)(2)(a) above shall, upon
conviction thereof, be subject to the penalty provided in §
10.99(A).
(Prior Code, § 4-2-6)
   (B)   (1)   Any person who does not abate the nuisance within the required
time period or who otherwise violates the provisions of §§
90.20 and
90.21 shall be subject to a penalty as provided in §
10.99(A) for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a nuisance continues unabated after ten days from
receipt of notice.
      (2)   In addition to any penalties herein provided, the village may issue
stop work orders upon owners of lots where work is unfinished under a
previously issued building permit for any violation of §§
90.20 and
90.21.
(Prior Code, § 4-3-3)
   (C)   Any person violating any provision of §
90.50 shall be fined not less than $250 nor more than $500 for each offense,
and a separate offense shall be deemed to be committed on each date during or
on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, § 4-7-3)
(Ord. passed 10-10-1992)
CHAPTER 91: ANIMALS
Section

91.25   Collars and tags

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 91.01 RUNNING AT LARGE PROHIBITED.
   (A)   No animal, including, but not limited to, a stallion, horse, mare,
gelding, colt, mule, ass, jenny, bull, ox, cow, heifer, steer, hog, shoat,
goat, chicken, duck or goose shall be allowed to run at large within the
village. Any such animal running at large in any public place in the village
shall be impounded.
   (B)   The herding of any animals or tying out of the same to graze upon any
street or alley of the village shall be deemed and taken as permitting the same
to run at large, within the meaning of this section. It is further unlawful to
picket or tie any such animal in any of the streets of the village for the
purpose of grazing or feeding.
§ 91.02 KEEPING OF SWINE, PIGS, LIVESTOCK, POULTRY PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful to keep any live swine or pigs, cattle, sheep, goat or
poultry anywhere in the village, except as may be specified by the Village
Board.
§ 91.03 CRUELTY TO ANIMALS.
   No person shall cruelly treat any animals in the village in any way. Any
person who inhumanely beats, underfeeds, overloads or abandons any animals
shall be deemed guilty of a violation of this section.
§ 91.04 DISEASED ANIMALS; REGULATIONS.
   (A)   No domestic animal afflicted with a contagious or infectious disease
shall be allowed to run at large, or to be exposed in any public place whereby
the health of humans or beast may be affected; nor shall such diseased animal
be shipped or removed from the premises of the owner thereof, except under the
supervision of the Chief of Police or the Health Officer.
   (B)   It is the duty of the Health Officer to secure such disposition of any
diseased animal and such treatment of affected premises as to prevent the
communication and spread of the contagion or infection, except in cases where
the state veterinarian is empowered to act.
§ 91.05 CONDITION OF PREMISES.
   No person shall cause or allow any stable or place where any animal is or
may be kept to become unclean or unwholesome.
§ 91.06 NOISY ANIMALS.
   It is unlawful to harbor or keep any animal which disturbs the peace by loud
noises at any time of the day or night.
§ 91.07 RELEASING ANIMALS FROM ENCLOSURE.
   No person shall willfully drive or entice any animal from beyond the village
limits into the village, or aid or abet the same, or let any animal out of an
enclosure in which it may be confined, or aid or abet in the letting out or
escape thereof, in order to take up and impound the same.
§ 91.08 ENCOURAGING ANIMALS TO FIGHT.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to be present at any dog fight within
the village when the animals shall meet accidentally, and by any act or word,
encourage or urge the dogs to fight. It shall further be unlawful for any
person to purposely or by agreement set any dogs, cocks or any other animals
whatsoever to fighting.
§ 91.09 REMOVAL OF DEAD ANIMALS.
   All dead animals in the village shall, within 12 hours of death of the same,
be removed and disposed of by the owner or keeper thereof, by burial or
cremation.
§ 91.10 DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   (A)   No person shall own, keep, or harbor within the village:
      (1)   Any snake, reptile or lizard that is physically capable of causing
bodily injury to any person or animal, regardless of age, by bite, poison,
constriction or other means;
      (2)   Any lion, tiger, cougar, jaguar, panther, bobcat, mountain lion,
lynx, ocelot, leopard, any hybrid thereof or any similar feline animal, bear,
exotic or wild animal not native to the state and which when fully grown will
attain a weight in excess of 30 pounds;
      (3)   Any wolf, coyote, jackal, fox, wild dog, or hybrid thereof;
      (4)   Any squirrels, opossums, raccoons, or skunks;
      (5)   Any birds of prey;
      (6)   Any monkey, gorilla, chimpanzee or other similar ape-like primate;
      (7)   Any bees, wasps, or hornets;
      (8)   Any farm animals; and/or
      (9)   Any other animals not allowed to be owned, kept or harbored under
any applicable local, state or federal law.
   (B)   Any animal listed in division (A) above is declared a public nuisance
and shall be removed within 30 days of the adoption of this section.
   (C)   This section shall not apply to properly zoned and constructed zoos,
veterinary hospitals, pounds, federally licensed exhibits, circuses, scientific
or research laboratories or educational or other licensed institutions.
   (D)   Notwithstanding the forgoing, in the discretion of the Village Board,
written permission may be granted upon application for special and limited
purpose to have a dangerous animal(s) present in the village for the following
limited purposes:
      (1)   Petting zoos;
      (2)   Pony rides;
      (3)   Zoos; and
      (4)   Circus.
   (E)   In addition to the penalties provided herein, any such dangerous
animal found within the village may be apprehended and impounded by the village
or its designee.
DOGS
§ 91.25 COLLARS AND TAGS.
   It shall be unlawful to permit any dog to be on any public street, sidewalk,
alley or other place unless such a dog has a collar firmly attached around his
neck with a county license tag for the current year attached to such collar.
§ 91.26 RUNNING AT LARGE PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful to permit any dog to run at large within the village.
Any dog found upon any public street, sidewalk, alley, parkway or any
unenclosed place shall be deemed running at large unless such dog is firmly
held on a leash or is in an enclosed vehicle.
§ 91.27 DOG BITES.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the owner or person having custody of any dog
which bites any human being to keep such dog locked up in an enclosure for a
period of two weeks following the time the dog has bitten a person; and to have
the dog examined by a physician or licensed veterinarian at the commencement
and termination of this two-week period. If such dog, during this period, shall
die or shall show definite signs of illness, it shall be the duty of such owner
or person having the dog to turn the dog over to the Health Officer or Chief of
Police so that it can be determined whether or not the dog had rabies.
   (B)   Members of the Police Department and Animal Control Officers, as
designated by the Village Board, are authorized to remove any dangerous dog to
a qualified veterinary service to destroy the animal. ANIMAL CONTROL OFFICER is
defined as any village employee authorized by the Village Board, including
Board members to carry out impoundment for the village.
§ 91.28 IMPOUNDMENT, REDEMPTION FEE.
   (A)   Any dog found upon any public street or highway running at large, or
any dog for which the annual tax has not been paid as hereinabove provided
shall be impounded by the Police Department. If such dog shall not have been
redeemed within five days after publication of notice of impoundment, it shall
be disposed of in the manner prescribed by the Board of Trustees.
   (B)   (1)   Any person seeking to redeem any impounded animal shall pay the
cost of impoundment and an additional fee of $50 for administrative cost charge
for the first offense, $100 for the second offense, and $200 for the third
offense, and also such person shall pay an additional charge for any additional
treatment, including, but not limited to, rabies shots, which are required for
every animal impounded.
      (2)   The administrative fee shall be paid to the Village Clerk. The
receipt will allow for release of such impounded dog, on payment of other fees.
      (3)   These fees include all court costs and attorney fees.
§ 91.29 ANIMAL DEFECATION CLEANUP.
   It shall be unlawful for anyone being the owner of or having charge of any
animal, to permit it to defecate, scratch or dig upon any public or private
property. The owner or person having charge of such animal shall clean up after
and remove any animal feces on such property and dispose of it immediately. It
shall be illegal to use the village sewer for disposal.
§ 91.30 KENNELS FOR BREEDING AND BOARDING PROHIBITED.
   No kennels for the breeding, boarding or selling of dogs or any other
animals for income shall be allowed within the village limits.
§ 91.31 NOISY DOGS.
   It is unlawful to harbor or keep any dog which disturbs the peace by loud
barking and noises at any time of the day or night.
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person or persons who shall violate any provision of this chapter
shall be fined not less than $50 nor more than $750 for each offense, plus
court cost and reasonable attorney fees incurred by the village in enforcing
this chapter.
   (B)   The penalty for violation of §
91.10 is $750 per offense. Each day any dangerous animal is kept in violation
of §
91.10 shall constitute a separate offense. It is not a defense to a violation
of §
91.10 that the owner or keeper of any such animal which is prohibited under §
91.10 has attempted to domesticate such animal.
CHAPTER 92: FIREWORKS
Section
   
92.01   Fireworks
   
92.02   Explosives
 
   
92.99   Penalty
§ 92.01 FIREWORKS.
   (A)   Storage. It shall be unlawful to store any fireworks in the village,
excepting as may be necessary for the giving of a licensed public exhibition of
pyrotechnics as hereinafter provided. Any such storage must be under the
supervision of and subject to the approval of the State Fire Marshal.
   (B)   Sales prohibited. It shall be unlawful to sell or offer for sale any
fireworks or pyrotechnics in the village.
   (C)   Discharge. It shall be unlawful to discharge or set off any fireworks
or pyrotechnics in the village; provided, that public exhibitions of fireworks
and pyrotechnics may be given if a permit therefor is granted by the Board.
Such exhibitions shall be given subject to the supervision of the State Fire
Marshal or of some person designated by him or her.
(Prior Code, § 4-6-1) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.02 EXPLOSIVES.
   (A)   Dynamite. It shall be unlawful to keep or store any dynamite in the
village in excess of five pounds on any one premises or in any one building.
   (B)   Nitroglycerine; T.N.T. It shall be unlawful to keep or store any
nitroglycerine or the explosive commonly known as T.N.T. in the village in any
quantities, excepting for medicinal or laboratory purposes, and for such
purpose, no more than one-half pound shall be stored in any one building or
premises.
   (C)   Gunpowder. It shall be unlawful to keep or store any black powder,
gunpowder, or guncotton in excess of five pounds on any one premises in the
village.
   (D)   Rules of State Fire Marshal. All explosives kept or stored in the
village shall be kept and stored in accordance with the rules enforced by the
State Fire Marshal with regard thereto.
(Prior Code, § 4-6-2)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall be subject
to penalty as provided in §
10.99(A).
(Prior Code, § 4-6-3)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 93.01 SHORT TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the “Fair Housing Ordinance
of the Village of Leaf River, Illinois” or “this chapter.”
(Prior Code, § 8-7-1) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.02 PURPOSE, DECLARATION OF POLICY.
   It is hereby declared to be the policy of the village and the purpose of
this chapter, in the exercise by the village of its policy and regulatory
powers for the protection of the public safety for the health, morals, safety,
and welfare of the persons in and residing in the village, and for the
maintenance and promotion of commerce, industry, and good government in the
village, to secure to all persons living or desiring to live in the village a
fair opportunity to purchase, lease, rent, or occupy housing without
discrimination based on race, color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-2) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.03 CONSTRUCTION OF CHAPTER.
   This chapter shall be constructed according to the fair import of its terms
and shall be liberally construed to further the purposes and policy stated in §
93.02 and the special purposes of the particular provision involved.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-3) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.04 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DWELLING. Any building or structure, or portion thereof, within the village,
which is arranged, designed, or used as a home, residence, or living quarters
of one or more individuals.
   HOUSING. Includes any building or structure or portion thereof, within the
village, which is used or occupied or is intended, arranged, or designed to be
used or occupied as the home, residence, or living quarters of one or more
individuals, groups, or families, and includes any vacant land within the
village which is zoned and intended to be used for construction of any such
building or structure.
   LEASE. Includes sublease, assignment, and rent (or rental), and includes any
contract to any of the foregoing.
   LENDING INSTITUTION. Any bank, insurance company, savings and loan
association, other persons in the business of lending money or guaranteeing
loans, any person in the business of obtaining, arranging, or negotiating loans
or guarantees as agent or broker, and any person in the business of buying or
selling loans or instruments for the payment of money which are secured by
title to a security interest in real estate, but shall not include any
religious institution or organization, nor any charitable or educational
organization operated, supervised, or controlled by a religious institution or
organization.
   NATIONAL ORIGIN. Includes the national origin of an ancestor.
   OWNER. Any person who holds legal or equitable title to, or owns any
beneficial interest in, any dwelling or housing, or who holds legal or
equitable title or share of, or holds any beneficial interest in any real
estate cooperative which owns any dwelling or housing.
   PERSON. Includes one or more individuals, corporations, partnerships,
associations, legal representatives, mutual companies, unincorporated
organizations, trust, trustees, trustees in bankruptcy, receivers, and
fiduciaries.
   PURCHASE. Includes any contract to purchase.
   REAL ESTATE AGENT. Any real estate broker, any real estate salesperson, and
any other person who as employee or agent or otherwise, engages in the
management or operation of any dwelling or housing.
   REAL ESTATE BROKER. Any person licensed as a real estate broker in
accordance with the provisions of the relevant Illinois Compiled Statutes, or
required thereby to be so licensed.
   REAL ESTATE SALESPERSON. Any person licensed as a real estate salesperson in
accordance with the provisions of the relevant Illinois Compiled Statutes, or
required thereby to be so licensed.
   REAL ESTATE TRANSACTION. The purchase, sale, exchange, or lease of any
dwelling or housing, and an option to do any of the foregoing.
   SALE. Includes any contract to sell, exchange or convey, transfer, or assign
legal or equitable title to, or a beneficial interest in, real estate.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-5) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.05 REPRESENTATION.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this chapter for
any person, for the purpose of inducing any other person to enter into a real
estate transaction with such person, his or her principal, or his or her agent:
   (A)   To represent that a change has occurred, will occur, or may occur with
respect to the race, color, religion, or national origin in the composition of
the owners or occupants in any block, neighborhood, or area in which the
dwelling or housing (which is the subject of the real estate transaction) so
located; or
   (B)   To represent that a change with respect to the race, color, religion,
or national origin in the composition of the owners or occupants in any block,
neighborhood, or area will result in lowering of property values, or in an
increase in criminal or antisocial behavior, or a decline in the quality of
schools, in such block, neighborhood, or area.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-8) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.06 OTHER VIOLATIONS.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this chapter for
any person:
   (A)   To aid, abet, incite, or coerce a person to engage in unlawful housing
practice;
   (B)   Willfully to interfere in the performance of a duty or the exercise of
a power by the Village Board or one of its member or representative; or
   (C)   Willfully to obstruct or prevent a person from complying with the
provisions of this chapter or an order issued thereunder.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-9) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.07 COMPLAINTS.
   (A)   Any person aggrieved in any manner by a violation of any provision of
this chapter may file with the Village Board a written verified complaint
setting forth his or her grievance. The complaint shall state:
      (1)   The name and address of the complainant;
      (2)   The name and address of the person against whom the complaint is
brought if known to the complainant; and
      (3)   The alleged facts surrounding the alleged violation of this
chapter, and such complaint shall state the name and address of all persons
believed to have knowledge concerning the alleged facts.
   (B)   After the filing of any complaint, the Village Board shall serve a
copy of the complaint on the party or parties charged.
   (C)   Any complaint filed hereunder with the Village Board must be filed
within 30 days after the alleged discriminatory practice occurred or it shall
be barred.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-10) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.08 HEARINGS.
   (A) Such hearing shall be conducted by the Village Board upon due and
reasonable notice to all parties. The Village Board shall have power to
administer oaths and to take sworn testimony. Any party alleged to have
violated this chapter shall be entitled to be represented by counsel and shall
have the right to call witnesses in his or her own behalf and to cross-examine
witnesses.
   (B)   At the conclusion of such hearing, the Village Board shall render the
complainant a decision.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-11) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.09 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   The Village Board shall be empowered to order any person found to be
engaging in an unfair housing practice to cease and desist from such practice,
upon such terms as shall be necessary and proper for the enforcement of this
chapter.
   (B)   The Village Board shall be empowered at the conclusion of the
proceedings held under §
93.08 to direct the Village Attorney to do any one or more of the following:
      (1)   To institute and prosecute proceedings to enforce, against any
person found in violation of this chapter, the fine provided for in §
93.99;
      (2)   To apply to any court of competent jurisdiction:
         (a)   For an order restraining any person from violating any provision
of this chapter; and
         (b)   For such other further relief as may seem to the court
appropriate for the enforcement of this chapter and the elimination of
violations hereof.
      (3)   To petition or institute proceedings with the Department of
Registration and education for the purpose of causing the Department to revoke,
suspend, or refuse to renew the license granted by such Department to any real
estate broker or real estate salesperson found to have violated any provision
of this chapter;
      (4)   In the case of any unlawful housing practice or violation of this
chapter by any person in the course of performing under a contract or
subcontract with the state or any political subdivision or agency thereof, or
with the United States of America or any agency or instrumentality thereof, to
petition or institute proceedings with such contracting agency for the purpose
of causing it to terminate such contract or any portion thereof, either
absolutely or on condition of compliance with the provisions of this chapter;
and
      (5)   The Village Board may issue such cease and desist orders and may
direct such action by the Village Attorney as shall be necessary for
enforcement of this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-12) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.10 REMEDIES.
   Any person aggrieved in any manner by the violation of any provision of this
chapter who has exhausted the remedies provided in §
93.08 may apply to any court of competent jurisdiction for appropriate relief
from such violation, including:
   (A)   An order compelling compliance with this chapter;
   (B)   An order to prohibit any person found by the court to have violated
any provision of this chapter from the sale, lease, exchange, transfer,
conveyance, or assignment of any dwelling or housing which is the subject of
such violation;
   (C)   An order requiring specific performance of any contract for the sale,
lease, exchange, transfer, conveyance, or assignment of any dwelling or
housing, or any person who, in violation of this chapter, refuses or fails to
perform such contract;
   (D)   Compensatory damages; and
   (E)   Such other and further relief as many seem to the court appropriate
for the enforcement of this chapter and the elimination of violations thereof.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-13) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
**** OWNER OF DWELLING, HOUSING ****
§ 93.25 DISCRIMINATORY TERMS.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this chapter for
any owner or other person to sell or lease a dwelling or housing on terms,
conditions, or privileges that discriminate between persons because of race,
color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-1) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.26 REFUSALS TO DEAL.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this chapter for
any owner or other person to refuse to negotiate for, enter into, or perform
any sale or lease of any dwelling or housing because of the race, color,
religion, or national origin of any party to such sale or lease, or any member
of the family of any such party, or of any person using or occupying or
intending to use or occupy such dwelling or housing, or of any person using or
occupying any dwelling or housing in the area in which such dwelling or housing
is located.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-2) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.27 WITHHOLDING HOUSING.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this chapter for
any owner or other person to represent to any person that any dwelling or
housing is not available for inspection, purchase, sale, lease, or occupancy
when in fact it is so available, or otherwise to withhold housing from any
person because of race, color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-3) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.28 ADVERTISEMENTS.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any owner or other person to publish or circulate a statement,
advertisement, or notice of an intention to sell or lease any dwelling or
housing in a manner that is unlawful under this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-4) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.29 SIGNS, NOTICES.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any owner or other person to post or erect, or cause any person to post or
erect, any sign or notice upon any dwelling or housing indicating an intent to
sell or lease any dwelling or housing in a manner that is unlawful under this
subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-5) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.30 EXEMPTIONS.
   This subchapter shall not apply to the rental of any room or dwelling unit
in any owner occupied dwelling which consists of more than one but less than
five dwelling units. As used herein, the term DWELLING UNIT means one or more
rooms which are arranged, designed, or used as living quarters for one family
or one individual. The term OWNER includes the spouse or any lineal descendant
or ancestor of the owner.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-6) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.31 LIMITATIONS.
   Nothing in this subchapter shall require any owner to offer property to the
public at large before selling or renting it; provided, he or she complies with
all other provisions of this subchapter. Nor shall this subchapter be deemed to
prohibit owners from giving preference to prospective tenants or buyers for any
reason other than religion, race, color, or national origin. Nothing in this
subchapter shall require an owner to offer property for sale or lease or to
show his or her property to any person if such person is not negotiating for
the purchase or lease of such property in good faith.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-6-7) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
**** REAL ESTATE AGENT FOR DWELLING, HOUSING ****
§ 93.45 WITHHOLDING HOUSING.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any real estate agent or other person to represent to any person that any
dwelling or housing is not available for inspection, sale, lease, or occupancy
when in fact it is so available, or otherwise to withhold housing from any
person because of race, color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-1) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.46 REFUSAL OF OFFERS.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any real estate agent or other person to refuse to receive or to fail to
transmit a bona fide offer for the purchase, sale, exchange, or lease of any
dwelling or housing because of race, color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-2) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.47 ADVERTISEMENTS.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any real estate agent or other person to publish or circulate a statement,
advertisement, or notice of an intention to sell or lease any dwelling or
housing in a manner that is unlawful under this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-3) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.48 SIGNS, NOTICES.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any real estate agent or other person to post or erect, or cause or permit
any person to post or erect, any sign or notice upon any dwelling or housing,
managed by or in the custody, care, or control of such real estate agent or
other person, indicating an intent to sell or lease any dwelling or housing in
a manner that is unlawful under §§
93.25 through
93.31 or this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-4) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.49 DISCRIMINATION IN LENDING.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any lending institution in making, agreeing to make, arranging, or
negotiating any loan or guarantee of funds for the purpose of financing the
purchase or sale, construction, lease rehabilitation, improvement, renovation,
or repair of any dwelling or housing, to offer, seek, or agree to terms,
conditions, or privileges that discriminate because of race, color, religion,
or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-5) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.50 REFUSALS TO DEAL IN LENDING.
   It shall be an unlawful housing practice and a violation of this subchapter
for any lending institution to refuse to negotiate for, enter into, or perform
any agreement to lend or guarantee the loan of funds for the purchase, sales
construction, lease rehabilitation, improvement, renovation, or repair of any
dwelling or housing because of race, color, religion, or national origin of any
party to such agreement or of any member of the family of any such party, or of
the residents of the area in which such dwelling or housing is located.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-6) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003) Penalty, see §
93.99
§ 93.51 COVERAGE.
   This subchapter shall apply, respectively, to every real estate agent who,
within the village, performs any function as such real estate agent, but does
not maintain an office or place of doing business within the village, and this
subchapter applies to every real estate agent and lending institution who
maintains an office or place of doing business within the village; provided,
however, that the provisions of this subchapter shall not be so construed as to
prohibit a real estate broker or real estate agent, on behalf of the owner,
from inquiring into and reporting upon qualifications of any prospective buyer
or tenant with respect to limitations or exclusions other than those of race,
color, religion, or national origin.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-7-7) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
§ 93.99 PENALTY.
   Any person who violates any provision of the chapter shall be subject to a
fine not to exceed $500.
(Prior Code, § 8-7-14) (Ord. 2003-5, passed 8-5-2003)
CHAPTER 94: PARKS AND PUBLIC PROPERTY
Section
   
94.01   Hours of use; use by children
   
94.02   Loitering, annoying acts
   
94.03   Damaging or destroying village park or property
   
94.04   Motor vehicles, bicycles, skateboards prohibited
   
94.05   Prohibited acts in village parks or Village Hall
§ 94.01 HOURS OF USE; USE BY CHILDREN.
   (A)   The hours that village parks and public grounds may be utilized by
members of the public are daily from dawn to dusk. No member of the public
shall be allowed in a village park or in any village grounds, place or building
after dusk or before dawn except with written permission from the Board of
Trustees of the village.
   (B)   At no time shall a child under the age of 18 be permitted in any
public park, public grounds or public building unless actively supervised by an
adult.
§ 94.02 LOITERING, ANNOYING ACTS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to loiter or lounge in or about any
village park, public grounds, place or building at any time for any immoral
purpose, or to the annoyance of others and to idly and without lawful business,
and with evident intent or purpose of annoyance, disturb or insult others by
any acts, gestures or language or for immoral purposes stand or be in and about
any of the village parks, village grounds, places or buildings, or be on any
public streets, alleys, walks or bridges or any other public places, to
obstruct or encumber any street crossing or any other public place of the
village by lounging or loitering in and about the same.
§ 94.03 DAMAGING OR DESTROYING VILLAGE PARK OR PROPERTY.
   It shall be unlawful to injure, tamper, break, destroy or deface, or assist
in injuring, breaking, destroying or defacing any public property in any public
park or on any public grounds within the village limits at any time.
§ 94.04 MOTOR VEHICLES, BICYCLES, SKATEBOARDS RESTRICTED.
   Except as for public roadways, motor vehicles and bicycles are prohibited in
village parks or other village property. Except as permitted by the proper
signage, all skateboards are prohibited in village parks or other village
property.
§ 94.05 PROHIBITED ACTS IN VILLAGE PARKS OR VILLAGE HALL.
   Smoking or possession of a firearm in village parks or on village property
is prohibited. Unless authorized by the Board of Trustees, possession of
alcohol in a village park or on village property is prohibited. Permitting a
dog in a village park or on village property without restraint by leash is
prohibited.

CHAPTER 110: BUSINESS LICENSES
Section
   
110.01   Purpose, jurisdiction
   
110.02   Definitions
   
110.03   Licenses required; exceptions
   
110.04   Application for license or permit
   
110.05   Investigation and inspections
   
110.06   Issuance or denial of license, permit
   
110.07   Forms of licenses, permits; signatures required
   
110.08   Term of license
   
110.09   License to be posted
   
110.10   Building and permits

110.13   Inter-track wagering prohibited
