150.66   Permit required; plan approval; fee
 
   
150.99   Penalty
**** BUILDING REGULATIONS ****
§ 150.01 BUILDING PERMIT REQUIRED.
   (A)   It is unlawful to construct or alter any building or structure in the
village where there are to be any changes in the height, outside dimensions, or
any structural changes, or where the bearing walls of any building or structure
are altered, or where a porch, breezeway, carport, or other roofed area is
enclosed, or construct a parking lot, without first securing a building permit.
No new building permit shall be issued unless the water connection charge,
water meter charge, and sewer connection charge are paid in full as set forth
in this code.
   (B)   (1)   Applications for such permits shall be made to the Village Clerk
and shall be accompanied by a completed permit application, two site plans and
two sets of building plans. The Building Inspector shall review the plans
within 14 days. Upon approval of the plans by the Building Inspector, the
Village Clerk will contact the applicant to pick up the permit. The applicant
must remit all permit fees, hookup fees, and impact fees prior to receiving the
approved building permit. All plans and papers shall be stamped “permit copy”.
The applicant shall sign the stamped required inspections sheet and stamped
electrical service handout. Upon payment of all fees, the applicant shall
receive a yellow copy and large hard copy of the permit to be posted. All plans
and papers shall be stamped “permit copy”.
      (2)   The following checklist shall facilitate application for a building
permit. This checklist does not supersede the above but shall be provided, upon
request, as a guide to the permit application process.
      (3)   Checklist for building permit application:
         (a)   Applicant completes building permit application;
         (b)   Applicant submits two site plans and two sets of building plans
along with a required fee as specified in the permit fee worksheet;
         (c)   The Village Clerk or designee creates a job file folder to be
kept on file at the Village Hall;
         (d)   The Village Clerk contacts the Building Inspector to review the
plans within 14 days and makes the required materials available to the Building
Inspector;
         (e)   At the completion of the review, the Building Inspector contacts
that Village Clerk when plans are ready to be issued;
         (f)   The Village Clerk contacts the applicant and notifies the
applicant that the plans are ready to be issued;
         (g)   All plans, required inspections, and the electrical service
handout must be stamped and signed prior to issuing the building permit;
         (h)   The applicant remits the permit fees, hookup fees and impact
fees prior to receiving the building permit;
         (i)   Upon payment of all outstanding fees, the Village Clerk issues a
yellow copy of the building permit and a large hard copy of the permit to be
posted; and
         (j)   All building permit application documents are to be kept in a
job folder and filed in the active file drawer.
   (C)   Such applications with the plans shall be referred to the Village
Building Inspector who shall examine the same to determine whether the proposed
preparations will comply with the ordinance provisions relating thereto. Upon
approval, one set of plans shall be returned by the Village Building Inspector
to the Village Clerk who shall issue a building permit. No permit shall be
issued except after approval of the plans and payment of the fee provided in
division (D) below.
   (D)   The fees for building permits shall be as set by the governing body.
When a building permit is required and work is started or proceeded with prior
to obtaining the same, the specified fee shall be doubled. The payment of such
double fee shall not relieve any person from fully complying with the
requirements of this code, nor from the completion of any work, nor from any
penalties prescribed herein.
   (E)   It is unlawful to vary materially from the approved plans and
specifications deposited with and approved by the Village Building Inspector
unless amended plans and specifications showing such proposed alteration or
variation are first filed with and approved by the Village Building Inspector.
   (F)   It shall be the duty of the Village Building Inspector and any
lawfully appointed deputies to enforce the provisions of this chapter. The
Village Building Inspector is empowered to make such inspections as may be
necessary to see to the enforcement of these provisions, and to make any tests
or examinations of materials or methods to be used, for the purpose of
determining if they comply with the requirements of this chapter.
   (G)   The permit holder must provide his or her name, phone number, and
address (including city) when calling for an inspection. The Building Inspector
will contact the permittee within 24 hours of notification to schedule an
inspection. If the Building Inspector needs to return more than once for the
same phase of any required inspection, an additional $75 fee will be required
to be paid prior to continuing construction. Work shall not proceed until the
Building Inspector approves various stages of construction.
   (H)   If the permit holder does not call for required inspections, the
Building Inspector may require the permit holder or contractor to remove or
open any necessary materials for the inspections to be completed. This will be
at the permit holder’s expense.
   (I)   A permit is good for one year unless the permittee fails to start
within six months. If the permittee starts within six months, but does not
complete the job within one year, the permittee can only get a (one) permit
extension for six months.
   (J)   A permit which has been expired for six months or less, may be renewed
provided no changes have been made in the original plans and specifications for
such work. The renewal fee shall be one-half of the amount required for a new
permit. Permits which have been expired for longer than six months require a
new application and payment of the full permit fee.
§ 150.02 PLANS, SPECIFICATIONS REQUIRED.
   No permit shall be issued for the construction of any building or structure,
or for remodeling or repairing affecting bearing walls or roofs, other than
resurfacing, or chimneys or for moving a building onto the premises in the
village, unless the application is accompanied by a plat or sketch of the
proposed location showing lot boundaries and by plans and specifications
showing the work to be done.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-2) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.03 COMPLIANCE WITH CODES; FEES.
   (A)   All work done under any permit issued shall be in full compliance with
the water ordinance, ordinances pertaining to street excavations, and all other
ordinances pertaining thereto.
   (B)   In addition to the fees for permits hereinafter provided for, there
shall be paid the fees prescribed in such ordinances, including:
 
Charge                       Fee
Building permit              $10
Sewer connection charge      $250
Water tap and connection fee $250
 
(Prior Code, § 8-1-3)
§ 150.04 ADMINISTRATION OF PROVISIONS.
   (A)   The proper village official shall have the power and duty to enforce
the provisions of this chapter, and all fees provided herein shall be paid to
the Village Clerk.
   (B)   All permits shall be issued by the Village Clerk.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-4)
§ 150.05 INTERPRETATION OF BUILDING REGULATIONS.
   Wherever in the building regulations it is provided that anything must be
done to the approval of or subject to the direction of any officer of the
village, this shall be construed to give such officer only the discretion of
determining whether the rules and standards established by ordinance have been
complied with, and no such provision shall be construed as giving any officer
discretionary powers as to what such regulations or standards shall be or power
to require conditions prescribed by ordinance or to enforce ordinance
provisions in an arbitrary or discriminatory manner.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-5)
§ 150.06 STOP WORK ORDER.
   (A)   Order issued.
      (1)   Whenever any work is being done in violation of the provisions of
this title, or in variance with the terms of any permit issued for such work,
the proper village official may order all work on the job stopped until such
violation or variance is eliminated and any work or installation made in
violation of this chapter corrected.
      (2)   Such stop order, if oral, shall be followed by a written stop order
within 24 hours (excluding Saturday, Sunday, or holidays).
   (B)   Performance of work after order. It shall be unlawful to do or perform
any work in violation of such stop order, except as may be necessary to prevent
injury or damage to persons or property.
   (C)   Revocation of order. Such stop order may be revoked by the Board of
Trustees.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-6) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.07 CERTIFICATE OF OCCUPANCY.
   No certificate of occupancy for any building or structure erected, altered,
or repaired after the effective date hereof shall be issued unless such
building or structure was erected, altered, or repaired in compliance with the
provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-7) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.08 USE OF PROPERTY.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      DWELLING. A building or portion of a building designed or used as the
living quarters of one or more families.
      LOT. A parcel of land intended to be separately owned, developed, and
otherwise used as a unit.
      MULTI-FAMILY DWELLING. A dwelling or group of dwellings on one lot
containing separate living units for two or more families, but which may have
joint services or facilities or both, but shall not include a hotel or motel.
   (B)   Prohibited. No building upon a lot contiguous to or adjacent to Main
Street within the village shall be used as a multi-family dwelling.
   (C)   Non-conforming uses. When the applicable provisions of this section do
not allow a use of part or all of a building lawfully existing as of the date
of adoption of this subchapter, such existing use may be continued so long as
otherwise lawful.
(Prior Code, § 8-1-8) (Ord. 2004-6, passed 11-5-2004) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.09 BUILDING CODE; MINIMUM REGULATIONS; AMENDMENTS AND REVISIONS.
   (A)   This code is supplemented by adoption of a building code establishing
minimum regulations, governing the design, construction, alteration,
enlargement, repair, demolition, removal, maintenance and use of all buildings
and structures defined by the 2006 International Code Council (ICC) books, 2006
International Property Maintenance Code book, 2000 NFPA 101 Life Safety Code
book and the 2005 NEC Handbook; providing for the issuance of permits,
collection of fees, making of inspections; providing penalties for the
violation thereof; to be known as the “building codes”; and repealing all
existing provisions of this code that are in conflict with the terms,
conditions and limitations of the 2006 ICC, 2000 NFPA and 2005 NEC Building
Codes. The following books have been adopted:
      (1)   2006 International Property Maintenance Code;
      (2)   2006 International Residential Code;
      (3)   2006 International Building Code;
      (4)   2006 International Plumbing Code;
      (5)   2006 International Fire Code;
      (6)   2006 International Mechanical Code;
      (7)   2000 NFPA 101 Life Safety Code; and
      (8)   2005 NEC Handbook.
   (B)   (1)   2006 International Property Maintenance Code is hereby amended
to delete sections: 107.2(5) and 107.2(6) regarding form of Notice; 108.3
regarding Notice; 110.1 through 110.3 regarding demolition; and 111 regarding
means of appeal. With regards to demolition of structures and notice of same,
the village shall comply with state statutes as amended.
      (2)   (a)   2006 International Residential Code is hereby amended to
delete Section R112 regarding Board of Appeals in its entirety.
         (b)   Section R105.2, “Work exempt from permit”, the first sentence of
same is hereby amended to read as follows, “Except where in conflict with the
Village Code of Ordinances, permits shall not be required for the following.”
The rest and remainder of Section R105.2 shall remain in full force and effect.
         (c)   Section 105.3.2, “Time limitation of application”, shall be
amended to delete the following language therein, “one or more extensions of
time for additional periods not exceeding 180 days each” and insert the
following language, “one extension for an additional period not exceeding 90
days.”
         (d)   Section R105.5, “Expiration”, as currently written is hereby
deleted in its entirety and henceforth shall read as follows, “Every permit
issued shall become invalid unless work authorized by such permit is commenced
within 180 days after its issuance, or if the work authorized by such permit is
suspended or abandoned for a period of 180 days after the time work is
commenced. The Board of Trustees, in its sole discretion, may grant one permit
extension for a period not more than 180 days. The extension shall be requested
in writing within thirty (30) days of the expiration of the initial permit and
justifiable cause demonstrated therein for the extension.”
         (e)   Section R113.2, “Notice of violation”, shall be amended to
provide that, “The building official and/or the municipal attorney are
authorized to serve notice of violation or order”. The rest of Section R113.2
shall remain in full force and effect.
      (3)   (a)   2006 International Building Code is hereby amended to delete
Sections 112 regarding Board of Appeals, and 115.4, “Method of Service” in
their entirety.
         (b)   Section 105.2, “Work exempt from permit”, the first sentence of
same is hereby amended to read as follows, “Except where in conflict with the
Village Code of Ordinances, permits shall not be required for the following.”
The rest and remainder of Section 105.2 shall remain in full force and effect.
         (c)   Section 105.3.2, “Time limitation of application”, shall be
amended to delete the following language therein, “one or more extensions of
time for additional periods not exceeding 90 days each” and insert therein the
following language, “one extension for an additional period not exceeding 90
days.”
         (d)   Section 105.5, “Expiration”, as currently written is hereby
deleted in its entirety and henceforth shall read as follows, “Every permit
shall become invalid unless the work on the site authorized by such permit is
commenced within 180 days after its issuance, or if the work authorized on the
site by such permit is suspended or abandoned for a period of 180 days after
the time the work is commenced. The Board of Trustees, in its sole discretion,
may grant one (1) permit extension of for a period not more than 180 days. The
extension shall be requested in writing within thirty (30) days of the
expiration of the initial permit and justifiable cause demonstrated therein for
the extension.”
         (e)   Section 1156.3, “Notice”, shall be amended to provide that, “If
an unsafe condition is found, the building official or the municipal attorney
shall serve notice on the owner, agent or person in control of the structure”.
The rest and remainder of Section 115.3 shall remain in full force and effect.
      (4)   Electrical Code amendments.
         (a)   Amendments. The following amendments are made to the published
form of the National Electrical Code, 2005 edition.
            1.   Meter bases shall be installed at a minimum of four feet to a
maximum of five feet six inches to the center of the meter at the finish grade
from top of finished patio and deck in front of meter.
            2.   All meter bases shall be installed outside, accessible to
meter reader, subject to village Electrical Inspector’s approval.
            3.   All new or replacement service entrance conductors shall be
installed in rigid metal and/or nonmetallic conduit.
            4.   All electrical wiring in industrial structures shall be
installed in electrical metal tubing (EMT), rigid intermediate metal conduit
(IMC), or ridged metal conduit (RMC). All electrical wiring for commercial and
multi-family buildings having more than three stories plus an elevator shall be
installed in EMT, IMC, or RMC. Where conduit is required and where there will
be low voltage wiring then the low voltage wiring shall be installed in EMT,
IMC, or RMC if in concealed spaces.
            5.   Duplexes, apartment houses, other multiple use buildings shall
have banked meters.
            6.   The National Electrical Code, 2005 edition, as adopted by the
village is applicable to mobile homes located within the corporate boundaries
of the village.
      (b)   Electrical equipment defined. “Electrical equipment”, as used in
this section, shall be construed to mean and include conductors and equipment
installed for the use of electricity supplied for light, heat or power, but
does not include radio apparatus or equipment for the wireless reception of
sounds and signals, nor ordinary household appliances such as toasters, vacuum
cleaners, or washing machines; and does not include apparatus, conductors and
other equipment installed for and by public utilities, including common
carriers, which are under the jurisdiction of the Illinois Commerce Commission
for use in their operations as public utilities.
§ 150.10 DEMOLITION PERMITS.
   No building or part of a building or accessory structure shall be demolished
in the village without a demolition permit issued by the Village Clerk.
Application for a demolition permit shall be obtained from the Village Clerk.
The village may charge a permit fee which fee shall be set by the Village Board
from time to time. Violation of this section shall be subject to a fine as
provided in the general penalty provision of the village code.
**** FENCES ****
§ 150.20 HEIGHT, LOCATION.
   (A)   No person shall build, construct, use, or maintain any fence or
barrier within the village along the line of or in or upon or along any street,
alley, or public walk or drive of a greater height than six feet above the
sidewalk grade or six feet above the surface of the ground where no grade is
established in any side or rear yard; provided, that visibility at right angles
to any surface of such fence not be reduced by more than 40%.
   (B)   No fence shall be built or erected between the front setback line of
the building and the front lot line, except natural fences of hedges, trees,
and shrubbery which shall be limited to 24 inches in height.
(Prior Code, § 8-2-1) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.21 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Quality and materials generally. All fences constructed in the village
shall be constructed in a good and workmanlike manner and of suitable material
such as lumber, stone, cyclone fencing material, or other durable building
material.
   (B)   Restricted or prohibited materials.
      (1)   Flimsy or unsuitable materials. Fences shall not be constructed of
flimsy or unsuitable building material, such as barrel staves, utility poles,
chicken coop wire, or other thin wire material and crating material.
      (2)   Barbed wire or injurious materials. Except as permitted in division
(B)(3) below, no person shall maintain or construct any fence composed, in
whole or in part, of barbed wire or with any similar material designed to cause
injury to any person or any wire charged with electric current anywhere within
three feet of any public street, sidewalk, alley, park, or other public way or
place.
      (3)   Location of injurious fence restricted. Except for fences around
industrial property, no person being the owner, lessee, or agent of any real
estate in the village shall erect, maintain, or permit to be erected or
maintained on or about the exterior building line of the real estate or upon
any portion of the sidewalk adjacent to such real estate any railing, fence,
guard, or protection of any kind of barbed wire, or upon which railing, fence,
guard, or other protection there shall be affixed or placed, or in any manner
attached, any spike, nail, or pointed instrument of any kind or description.
Each and every day such person shall fail or neglect to remove from such
railing, fence, or other protection any such spike, nail, or other pointed
instrument after notice, in writing, from the Police Department or other
village official to do so, he or she shall be deemed guilty of a new, separate,
and distinct offense.
(Prior Code, § 8-2-2) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.22 FENCE PERMITS.
   (A)   No fence shall be erected or placed upon any property without first
obtaining a permit from the village.
   (B)   There is a $10 charge for a fence permit.
   (C)   Fences constructed prior to the adoption hereof, which do not conform
to the provisions of this subchapter, shall be considered legal nonconforming
structures. An existing fence may be maintained, repaired or structurally
altered, however, no such repair or structural alteration shall create an
additional nonconformity or increase the degree of nonconformity.
§ 150.23 SPECIFICATIONS, CONDITIONS AND TYPES.
   (A)   (1)   A fence, including all posts, bases and other structural parts,
shall be located completely within the boundaries of the lot on which it is
located. No fence shall be located closer than 24 inches to a public sidewalk.
The construction of all fences shall comply with all applicable covenants and
restrictions. No fence shall be constructed if prohibited by private covenants
or restrictions. It shall be the responsibility of the applicant, not the
village, to ascertain the existence of applicable covenants and restrictions.
The issuance of a fence permit and/or the subsequent construction of fences in
violation of applicable covenants and restrictions shall not stop the village
or other parties from bringing suit for injunction seeking removal of said
fence.
      (2)   Fences shall be designed and constructed to resist a horizontal
wind pressure of not less than 30 pounds per square foot.
      (3)   All fences shall be constructed with the finished surface facing
neighboring property with support posts placed to the inside, except in cases
where the posts are an integral part of the fence design which enhances the
aesthetic appearance of the fence.
      (4)   Except for corner lots and the rear yard of a through lot, every
fence which creates a wholly enclosed area and adjoins an existing or proposed
street right of way, shall include a gate to provide access to the right-of-
way. Corner lots and rear yards of through lots shall provide a point of
ingress and egress to the property.
      (5)   No fence shall be erected in such a manner as to limit or obstruct
the sight distance of motorists entering or leaving an intersection or alley.
      (6)   Decorative fences (i.e., one- or two-split rail type) shall be
allowed in front yards, provided the fence is not on village right-of-way and
does not exceed four feet in height.
      (7)   The maximum height for side yard or back yard fences shall not
exceed six feet, in addition to an opening between the bottom of the fence and
grade not to exceed six inches.
      (8)   Barbed wire, razor wire, concertina wire or electrification of
fences is prohibited.
      (9)   Fences for lots with swimming pools shall conform to the
regulations hereof governing private swimming pools.
   (B)   The minimum setback for privacy and chain link fences shall be 18
inches from the adjoining property line, sidewalk, or street or alley way when
no sidewalk is present, and shall not be placed so as to block the view of
oncoming traffic at a corner lot or lot adjacent to an alleyway.
§ 150.24 RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNERS AND OCCUPANTS; EXEMPTIONS.
   (A)   (1)   It shall be the responsibility of the owner and/or occupant of
the property where a fence is erected to maintain the structure in good repair
at all times.
      (2)   If a fence is found to be in a deteriorated condition and/or in
need of repair, including, but not limited to, broken or missing structural
components, and/or the fence is substantially less than perpendicular to grade,
the village after consulting with the Building Inspector, may order the fencing
to be repaired, replaced or removed depending on the condition of the fence.
      (3)   Such order shall be in writing.
   (B)   The provisions of this subchapter shall not apply to fences, walls or
shrubbery owned or maintained by the village, or to fences constructed or
maintained by any other governmental body or agency for which the principal
purpose is inherent to public safety.
**** DANGEROUS BUILDINGS ****
§ 150.35 PROPERTY MAINTENANCE CODE ADOPTED.
   (A)   The chapters of the most current edition of the ICC Property
Maintenance Code Book (2006) are hereby adopted by the village to establish
minimum standards for the maintenance of all buildings within the village
regardless of the date of their construction. Where the Property Maintenance
Code Book and the village ordinances conflict, the Property Maintenance Code
Book shall control.
   (B)   Three copies of the said ICC Property Maintenance Code Book (2006),
marked as Exhibits A, B, and C, are incorporated herein by reference and will
remain on file in the office of the Village Clerk for public inspection.
(Ord. 2014-2, passed 8-5-2014)
§ 150.36 NUISANCE DECLARED.
   (A)   All buildings or portions thereof which are determined to be a
dangerous building, as defined herein, and which constitute a hazard to the
public health, safety, and welfare are hereby declared to be public nuisances
and shall be abated by repair, rehabilitation, demolition, or removal in
accordance with the procedures specified in §§
90.01 through
90.05.
   (B)   The village may require the vacating or relocation of occupants of any
such dangerous building and securing thereof.
(Prior Code, § 8-3-2)
**** MOVING BUILDINGS ****
§ 150.50 PERMIT REQUIRED; APPLICATION.
   (A)   No person shall move any building from one lot to another lot, or on
or over any street, alley, sidewalk, or other public place or way in the
village without a permit for the same from the Board of Trustees.
   (B)   Applications for permits shall be made, in writing, to the Clerk and
shall state thereon the proposed route and the number of days it is intended
that the building shall occupy any public way or place.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-1)
§ 150.51 BOND REQUIREMENTS.
   Every person applying for a permit under this subchapter shall submit with
his or her application a bond with corporate sureties to be approved by the
President and Board of Trustees, conditioned on compliance with all the
provisions of this subchapter and agreeing to pay and hold the village harmless
from any claim which may be made against it by reason of the occupation, by the
building or structure moved, of any street, sidewalk, alley, or other public
place or way.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-2)
§ 150.52 APPROVAL OF ROUTES; FEES, ISSUANCE OF PERMIT.
   (A)   Upon approval of the intended route by the Committee on Streets and
Alleys, a fee of $50 plus $100 for each day it is intended that such building
shall occupy such portion of any public way or place, shall be paid to the
Village Clerk and a permit issued.
   (B)   An additional fee of $100 for each day, or fraction thereof, over and
above the time stated in the application, shall be paid for occupying such way
or place.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-3)
§ 150.53 LIGHTS, WARNINGS.
   (A)   Whenever a street or alley is blocked by a house or structure which is
being moved, warnings to the effect shall be placed by the contractor so as to
warn vehicles and persons from entering that portion of the street which is so
blocked.
   (B)   The person moving any building through the streets shall keep warning
signs and lights at night on the building or structure so as to guard against
any person or vehicle colliding with it.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-4)
§ 150.54 CUTTING OF WIRES.
   (A)   Whenever it shall be necessary to interfere with the wires or cables
of a public utility in moving a building, the terms of any special franchise
ordinance shall apply and the bond specified therein given.
   (B)   If no such terms apply, then the President and Board of Trustees shall
estimate the expense of replacing the wires, and the bond to be given to the
village shall cover such expense.
(Prior Code, § 8-4-5)
**** BROADCAST RECEIVING ANTENNAS ****
§ 150.65 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BROADCAST RECEIVING ANTENNA. An outside satellite dish used for the
reception of signals transmitted by stations licensed by the Federal
Communications Commission and the Radio Broadcast Services, including A.M.,
F.M., and television.
   COMMERCIAL BUSINESS. Any building or premises located in the commercial
district of the village.
   COMMERCIAL PURPOSE. The use, operation, or maintenance of any broadcast
receiving antenna for the purpose of advertising any business or any services,
or for the purpose of attracting the attention of the public to, or advertising
for or soliciting patronage or customers to or for any performance, show,
entertainment, exhibition, or event, or for the purpose of demonstrating any
such broadcast receiving antenna.
   FRONT YARD. The area required between the street and the front of the home
or other building on the property having a depth of not less than 15% of the
lot depth.
   LOT CORNER. A lot situated at the intersection of two or more streets.
   NON-COMMERCIAL PURPOSE. The use, operation, or maintenance of any broadcast
receiving antenna for other than a commercial purpose.
   SIDE YARD. The area required on each side of the buildings located on the
lot with a width of not less than 10% of the lot width.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-1) (Ord. passed 5-7-1985)
§ 150.66 PERMIT REQUIRED; PLAN APPROVAL; FEE.
   (A)   Purpose. The permit procedure shall be for the purpose of ensuring
that an antenna is installed in conformance to the requirements of this
subchapter.
   (B)   Submittal of plans; permit issuance. It shall be unlawful for any
person to erect or cause to be erected within the village any broadcast
receiving antenna without first submitting plans for such antenna to the
Village Board for approval, and it shall issue a permit for the erection of an
antenna complying with the provisions of this subchapter.
   (C)   Permit fee. The permit fee shall be $10.
   (D)   Standards for approval. The Village Board shall be guided by the
following standards in the approval of the antenna plans.
      (1)   No part of the broadcast receiving antenna structure shall extend
to a height of more than 15 feet above ground level.
      (2)   No part of the broadcast receiving antenna structure shall be
located in the front yard nor on any easements.
      (3)   Where a broadcast receiving antenna is to be placed on a lot
corner, no part of the broadcast receiving antenna structure shall be placed in
the side yard.
      (4)   A broadcast receiving antenna to be placed in a commercial district
may be placed on the roof of the commercial business.
      (5)   A broadcast receiving antenna to be placed in a residential
district shall only be installed for a non-commercial purpose and serve only
the property on which installation is located and contiguous property.
(Prior Code, § 8-5-2) (Ord. passed 5-7-1985) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Upon conviction of an offense alleging a violation of §§
150.35 and
150.36, a person is subject to a penalty as provided in §
10.99(A).
(Prior Code, § 8-3-3)
   (C)   Any person violating any provision of §§
150.50 through
150.54 shall be subject to penalty as provided in §
10.99(A).
(Prior Code, § 8-4-6)
CHAPTER 151: MOBILE HOMES, PARKS
Section
   
151.01   Definitions
   
151.02   Licensing requirements for mobile home parks
   
151.03   Site requirements
   
151.04   Operation of parks
   
151.05   Removal of wheels
   
151.06   Parking, storage of mobile home outside park
   
151.07   Right of access
§ 151.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   MOBILE HOME. Any vehicle or similar portable structure constructed so as to
permit its being used as a conveyance on a public street and so as to permit
the occupancy thereof as a dwelling by one or more persons.
   MOBILE HOME PARK. An area of land on which two or more occupied mobile homes
are harbored, either free of charge or for revenue, together with any building,
structure, or enclosure used as part of the equipment of such park.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-1)
§ 151.02 LICENSING REQUIREMENTS FOR MOBILE HOME PARKS.
   (A)   License required. It shall be unlawful to establish, maintain, or
operate any mobile home park in the village without first having obtained a
license therefor.
   (B)   Application for license.
      (1)   Application for such license shall contain the name of the
applicant, the location of the proposed park, and the number of mobile homes to
be accommodated.
      (2)   Each such application shall be accompanied by a plat or sketch
showing the size and location of all buildings and structures.
   (C)   License fee. The license fee is $50 per year. Whenever the number of
such accommodations is increased, this shall be reported to the Village Clerk,
and the additional fee, if any, necessitated by such increase, shall be paid
before the additional spaces are put to use. When a license is applied for or
accommodations are increased during the license year, the fee shall be prorated
on the basis of the number of months remaining in the license year.
   (D)   Restrictions on issuance of license. No such license shall be issued
to any but a person of good character, nor to any corporation if any officer
thereof is not a person of good character.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-2) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 151.03 SITE REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Each mobile home shall be allotted a site of not less than 2,500
square feet, exclusive of common driveways. No mobile home shall be parked
closer than seven and one-half feet to the side lot lines of a mobile home park
if the abutting property is improved property or closer than 15 feet to a
public street, alley, or building. Each individual mobile home site shall abut
or face on a common driveway or clear unoccupied space of not less than 22 feet
in width, which space shall have unobstructed access to a public highway.
   (B)   There shall be an open space of not less than 15 feet between the
sides of every mobile home and at least ten feet between the ends of every
mobile home. No mobile home shall occupy more than 25% of the mobile home site.
Parking for private automobiles shall be limited to a central parking area.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-3) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 151.04 OPERATION OF PARKS.
   (A)   Compliance with state regulations. It shall be the duty of the
licensee and person in charge of a mobile home park to comply with the
provisions of the State Mobile Home and Mobile Home Park Act, being 210 ILCS
115/1 et seq., and the regulations of the State Department of Health.
   (B)   Park manager. It shall be unlawful to hire or keep as manager,
superintendent, or person in charge of a mobile home park any person who is not
a person of good character or any person who has
been convicted of a felony.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-4) Penalty, see §
10.99
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 210 ILCS 115/1 et seq.
§ 151.05 REMOVAL OF WHEELS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to remove the wheels or other
transporting device from any mobile home or otherwise to affix a mobile home
permanently to the ground so as to prevent ready removal of such mobile home
unless a permit to do so is obtained as required for the construction of a new
building. Any such alteration shall be construed as converting the mobile home
into a building and subject to the requirements of the building ordinances of
the village.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful to occupy for sleeping or other residence
purposes any mobile home which has been rendered immobile by the removal of
wheels or placing the same on a foundation or on the ground unless such mobile
home, in construction and location, complies with the ordinances of the village
relating to the construction, wiring, plumbing, sewer facilities, and other
regulations applicable to single-family dwellings.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-5) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 151.06 PARKING, STORAGE OF MOBILE HOME OUTSIDE PARK.
   (A)   Parking outside park. It shall be unlawful for any person to maintain
any mobile home used for human habitation upon any plot of ground in the
village, except in a licensed mobile home park.
   (B)   Storage of mobile homes. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to
prohibit the storage of any mobile home for any length of time when the mobile
home is not used for living or sleeping purposes.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-6) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 151.07 RIGHT OF ACCESS.
   The members of the Police Department and other village officials shall have
access at any time to all parts of a mobile home park to ascertain compliance
with all village ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 8-6-7)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 152.01 SUPERVISION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   All public streets, alleys, sidewalks, and other public ways in the village
shall be under the supervision of the Committee on Streets and Alleys. It shall
have the supervision over all the work thereon and the cleaning thereof and
shall be charged with the enforcement of all ordinance provisions relating to
such public places (except traffic ordinances of the village) and is hereby
authorized to enforce such village ordinances.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-1)
Cross-reference:
   Traffic code, see Title VII
§ 152.02 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Permit required; application.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful to construct or lay any pavement on any public
street, sidewalk, alley, or other public way or to repair the same without
having first secured a permit therefor.
      (2)   Applications for such permits shall be made to the Village Clerk
and shall state the location of the intended pavement or repair, the extent
thereof, and the person who is to do the actual construction work. It shall be
the duty of the Village Clerk to issue the necessary permit if it appears that
the terms of this chapter are being complied with.
   (B)   Bond requirements. Each applicant shall furnish a cash bond or a bond
with a corporate surety in such amount not less than $1,000 nor more than
$100,000, as may be determined and approved by the Chairperson of the Committee
on Streets and Alleys or by the President and Board of Trustees, conditioned to
indemnify the village for any liability, loss, or damage resulting from the
work undertaken or the manner of doing same, and shall furnish evidence
acceptable to the Chairperson of the Committee on Streets and Alleys or the
President and Board of Trustees that the person who is to do the actual
construction work has in force satisfactory public liability and property
damage insurance in limits of at least $300,000 for injuries to each person,
$1,000,000 for injuries in each accident, and $100,000 for damage to property
in each accident covering all damages occasioned by or in any manner resulting
from such construction work.
   (C)   Conformance with specifications. All street and sidewalk pavements
shall be made in conformity with the specifications then currently on file in
the office of the Village Clerk.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-2) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.03 BARRICADES AND WARNING LIGHTS.
   (A)   Barricades and lights required.
      (1)   Any person laying or repairing any pavement on a street, sidewalk,
or other public place, or making an excavation in any such place, shall
maintain suitable barricades to prevent injury to any person or vehicle by
reason of such work; such barricades shall be protected by lights at nighttime.
      (2)   Any defect in any such pavement shall be barricaded to prevent
injury, and any person properly maintaining any such opening or excavation
while the same remains open shall guard it by proper barricades and lights.
   (B)   Disturbing barricades or lights. It shall be unlawful to disturb or
interfere with any barricades or lights lawfully placed to protect or mark any
new pavement or excavation or opening in any public street, alley, or sidewalk.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-3) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.04 RESPONSIBILITY FOR SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   Construction. It shall be the responsibility of the lot owner to
construct the initial sidewalk across his or her property.
   (B)   Maintenance and repairs. It shall be the responsibility of the village
to maintain, repair, and replace said sidewalks when deemed necessary by the
Chairperson of the Committee on Streets and Alleys, except those sidewalks that
are part of the lot owners’ driveways.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-4)
§ 152.05 ERECTION OF POLES AND WIRES.
   It shall be unlawful to erect any poles or wires or to maintain any poles or
wires on or over any public street, alley, or other public place without having
first secured a permit therefor from the President and Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-5) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.06 OPENINGS IN PUBLIC WAYS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to construct or maintain any opening or stairway
in any public street, sidewalk, or alley without a permit therefor from the
President and Board of Trustees.
   (B)   All such lawfully maintained openings shall be guarded by a suitable
strong cover or railing or such construction as will meet the approval of the
Committee on Streets and Alleys.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-6) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.07 PROHIBITED ACTS AND CONDITIONS.
   (A)   Damage to pavement. It shall be unlawful to damage any sidewalk,
street, or alley pavement.
   (B)   Obstructions; encroachments. It shall be unlawful for any person to
cause, create, or maintain any obstruction to any street, alley, sidewalk, or
other public way, except as may be specifically authorized by ordinance or by
the Committee on Streets and Alleys when necessary in an emergency or in
connection with any lawful construction, repair, or removal work. It shall
further be unlawful to obstruct any drain in any public street or alley.
(Prior Code, § 7-1-7) Penalty, see §
10.99
**** DRIVEWAYS ****
§ 152.20 PERMIT REQUIRED; FEES.
   No person shall construct a driveway for vehicles or animals across any
sidewalk or parkway in the village without first having obtained a permit
therefor, and the fee for such construction permits shall be $5.
(Prior Code, § 7-2-1) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.21 APPLICATION FOR PERMIT; ISSUANCE.
   Applications for such permits shall be made to the Village Clerk and shall
be accompanied by the required fee. It shall be the duty of the Village Clerk
to issue the necessary permit for construction of a driveway.
(Prior Code, § 7-2-2)
§ 152.22 CONSTRUCTION SPECIFICATIONS.
   (A)   Grade surface. No driveway shall be so constructed or graded as to
leave a step, sharp depression, or other obstruction in the sidewalk. The grade
shall be nearly as possible the same as that of the adjoining sidewalk. It
shall be unlawful to have the surface finish of any driveway where the same
crosses the sidewalk constructed of such materials as to render it slippery or
dangerous or hazardous to pedestrians or to have the grade of such portion vary
from the grade of the sidewalk or be other than level.
   (B)   Conformance to specifications. Driveways shall be constructed as per
specifications on file in the office of the Village Clerk.
(Prior Code, § 7-2-3) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.23 REPAIRS AND MAINTENANCE.
   It shall be the duty of the person maintaining a driveway to keep the same
in good repair where it crosses the sidewalk and free from obstructions and
openings.
(Prior Code, § 7-2-4)
**** EXCAVATIONS ****
§ 152.35 PERMIT REQUIRED; FEES.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to make or cause to be made any excavation in or
across or to tunnel under the curb, gutter, or roadway of any street or any
village property within the corporate limits of the village without having
first secured a permit therefor from the Village Clerk.
   (B)   A fee for such permit shall, in all cases, be $25 and shall be payable
with the application required by §
152.36.
(Prior Code, § 7-3-1) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.36 APPLICATION FOR PERMIT; BOND, ISSUANCE.
   (A)   Application required. Prior to doing any work involving an excavation
in or across or tunneling under the curb, guitar, or roadway of any street or
any village property, the person desiring to do the same shall file or cause to
be filed with the Village Clerk an application for a permit therefor.
   (B)   Contents of application. The application shall state who the applicant
is and where he or she resides, on whose behalf the work is to be done, the
nature of the work that is desired to be done, and the reason therefor, the
size and location of the street surface or other improvement to be injured or
torn up, and the exact location where the work is to be done.
   (C)   Review of application; issuance. Thereupon, the Village Clerk shall
transmit such application to the Chairperson of the Committee on Streets and
Alleys, and said Committee shall, without delay, investigate the propriety of
the applicant, and if it finds that a permit for such work should be issued, it
shall direct the Village Clerk to issue said permit on payment of required
fees.
   (D)   Bond requirements. The applicant shall be required to enter into a
cash bond or a bond with a corporate surety in such amount not less than $1,000
nor more than $10,000, conditioned that the applicant will do the work for
which he or she has applied for a permit in a proper and workmanlike manner,
and in accordance with the specifications set forth in §
152.37, that the applicant will pay any and all damages to the street surface
or improvement that may arise due to operations of the applicant and that the
applicant will pay any and all damages that may be recovered against the
village to any person or property occasioned by or in any manner resulting from
the operations of the applicant on public property, such bond to be effective
for a duration of one year after the date on which the applicant has ceased his
or her operations.
   (E)   Liability insurance. The person who is to do the actual construction
work will have in force satisfactory public liability and property damage
insurance in limits of at least $300,000 for injuries to each person,
$1,000,000 for injuries in each accident, and $100,000 for damage to property
in each accident, covering all damages occasioned by or in any manner resulting
from such construction work.
(Prior Code, § 7-3-2)
§ 152.37 REPAIR, RESTORATION OF STREET SURFACE.
   Any person doing any work involving an excavation in or across, or the
tunneling under, the curb, gutter, or roadway of any street or any village
property, pursuant to the permit referred to in §§
152.35 and
152.36 shall promptly restore any street surface or other improvement which he
or she may injure or tear up. Street surfaces so injured or torn up shall be
restored in accordance with the specifications currently on file in the office
of the Village Clerk.
(Prior Code, § 7-3-3)
§ 152.38 LIABILITY FOR DAMAGES.
   Whoever shall injure or tear up, for any purpose whatsoever, any pavement of
any street, alley, sidewalk, curb, or gutter, or any part thereof or dig any
hole, trench, ditch, or drain, or dig or remove any sod, stone, curb, earth,
sand, or gravel from any street, alley, sidewalk, parkway, or other public
property, shall be responsible and liable to the village for all damages to
persons or property in consequence thereof which the village shall suffer or be
adjudged to pay.
(Prior Code, § 7-3-4)
**** OVERHANGING SIGNS, AWNINGS ON STREETS, SIDEWALKS ****
§ 152.50 PERMITS, FEES.
   (A)   Permit required. No person shall erect or maintain any sign or
signboard or rigid canopy over any street, alley, sidewalk, or other public way
in the village without having first obtained a permit therefor as is herein
provided. Permits for such signs or canopies shall be issued by the Village
Clerk upon the payment of a fee of $10 and shall designate the location of the
proposed sign or canopy.
   (B)   Temporary permit. Temporary permits may be issued for the maintenance
of a temporary sign or canopy for a short time, upon the payment of a fee of $5
for each day or fraction thereof that such sign or canopy may be maintained.
(Prior Code, § 7-4-1) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 152.51 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS.
   All signs and canopies extending over any sidewalk, street, alley, or other
public place shall be securely fastened and constructed.
(Prior Code, § 7-4-2)
§ 152.52 HEIGHT ABOVE WALK.
   The lowest part of any sign or canopy or of any non-rigid awning or support
thereof which extends over any public way shall be at least eight feet above
the level of the walk or public way over which it extends, but no such sign
shall be maintained over any public way used by vehicles if any part of it or
of its support is less than 15 feet above the level of such public way.
(Prior Code, § 7-4-3)
§ 152.53 INSPECTIONS.
   The Committee on Streets and Alleys may inspect, at regular intervals, every
sign, canopy, or awning which extends over any sidewalk, street, alley, or
other public way. If any such sign or canopy is found to be insecurely
fastened, the member of such Committee making such inspection shall report this
fact to the owner of the sign, canopy, or awning or to the owner or occupant of
the premises on which it is fastened, and such owner or occupant shall either
repair or remove such sign, canopy, or awning within ten days thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 7-4-4)
CHAPTER 153: TREES
Section
   
153.01   Nuisance trees
   
153.02   Removal of village-owned trees
§ 153.01 NUISANCE TREES.
   (A)   Dutch Elm diseased trees. Trees of all species and varieties of ulmus
(elm) and zelkova infected with the fungus Ceratostomella ulmi, as determined
by laboratory analysis, are hereby declared to be a public nuisance and shall
be removed and burned within ten days following notification of the discovery
of such infection. It shall be unlawful for any person owning property whereon
such a tree is situated to possess or keep such a tree after the expiration of
ten days following notification of the discovery of said infection.
   (B)   Elm Bark Beetle breeding places. Trees or parts thereof of ulmus (elm)
or zelkova in a dead or dying condition that may serve as a breeding place for
the European elm bark beetle, Scolytus multistristus, are hereby declared to be
public nuisances, and it shall be unlawful for the person owning property
whereon the same is situated to possess or keep the same.
(Prior Code, § 4-4-1) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 153.02 REMOVAL OF VILLAGE-OWNED TREES.
   Trees on village-owned property requiring removal shall be removed at the
expense of the village.
(Prior Code, § 4-4-2)
CHAPTER 154: FLOOD CONTROL REGULATIONS

154.10   Preventing increased damages

§ 154.01 PURPOSE.
   This chapter is enacted pursuant to the police powers granted to this
village by 65 ILCS 5/1-2-1, 5/11-12-12, 5/11-30-2, 5/11-30-8, and 5/11-31-2 in
order to accomplish the following purposes:
   (A)   To prevent unwise development from increasing flood or drainage
hazards to others;
   (B)   To protect new buildings and major improvements to buildings from
flood damage;
   (C)   To protect human life and health from the hazards of flooding;
   (D)   To lessen the burden on the taxpayer for flood control projects,
repairs to flood-damaged public facilities, and utilities and flood rescue and
relief operations;
   (E)   To maintain property values and a stable tax base by minimizing the
potential for creating flood blighted areas; and
   (F)   To make federally subsidized flood insurance available for property in
the village by fulfilling the requirements of the National Flood Insurance
Program.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-1) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BASE FLOOD. The flood having a 1% probability of being equaled or exceeded
in any given year. The BASE FLOOD is also known as the 100-YEAR FLOOD. The base
flood elevation at any location is as defined in §
154.04.
   BUILDING. A structure that is principally above ground and is enclosed by
walls and a roof. The term includes a gas or liquid storage tank, a
manufactured home, or prefabricated building. The term also includes
recreational vehicles and travel trailers to be installed on a site for more
than 180 days.
   DEVELOPMENT.
      (1)   Any human-made change to real estate, including:
         (a)   Construction, reconstruction, or placement of a building, or any
addition to a building valued at more than $1,000;
         (b)   Installing a manufactured home on a site, preparing a site for a
manufactured home, or installing a travel trailer on a site for more than 180
days;
         (c)   Installing utilities, construction of roads, or similar
projects;
         (d)   Construction or erection of levees, walls, fences, bridges, or
culverts;
         (e)   Drilling, mining, filling, dredging, grading, excavating, or
other non-agricultural alterations of the ground surface;
         (f)   Storage of materials; or
         (g)   Any other activity that might change the direction, height, or
velocity of flood or surface waters.
      (2)   DEVELOPMENT does not include maintenance of existing buildings and
facilities such as re-roofing, re-surfacing roads, or gardening, plowing, and
similar agricultural practices that do not involve filling, grading, or
construction of levees.
   FPE or FLOOD PROTECTION ELEVATION. The elevation of the base flood plus one
foot at any given location in the SFHA.
   FLOOD. A general and temporary condition of inundation of normally dry land
areas from the overflow, the unusual and rapid accumulation or the runoff of
surface waters from any source.
   FLOODWAY. The portion of the SFHA required to store and convey the base
flood. The FLOODWAY for each of the SFHAs of the village shall be according to
the best data available to the State Water Survey Floodplain Information
Repository.
   RIVERINE SFHA. Any SFHA subject to flooding from a river, creek,
intermittent stream, ditch, or any other identified channel. This term does not
include areas subject to flooding from lakes (except public bodies of water),
ponding areas, areas of sheet flow, or other areas not subject to overbank
flooding.
   SFHA or SPECIAL FLOOD HAZARD AREA.
      (1)   Those lands within the jurisdiction of the village that are subject
to inundation by the base flood. The SFHAs of the village are generally
identified as such on the Flood Insurance Rate Map of the village prepared by
the Federal Emergency Management Agency and dated June 18, 1987.
      (2)   The SFHAs of those parts of the unincorporated county that are
within the extraterritorial jurisdiction of the village or that may be annexed
into the village are generally identified as such on the flood map prepared for
the county by the Federal Emergency Management Agency and dated March 10, 1978.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-2) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.03 ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   The proper village official shall be responsible for the general
administration and enforcement of this chapter, including, but not limited to,
the following duties:
   (A)   Ensure that all development activities within the SFHAs of the
jurisdiction of the village meet the requirements of this chapter;
   (B)   Provide information and assistance to citizens upon request about
permit procedures and floodplain construction techniques;
   (C)   Ensure that construction authorization has been granted by the State
Department of Transportation, Division of Water Resources, for all development
projects subject to §
154.10 and maintain a record of such authorization;
   (D)   Maintain a record of the as-built elevation of the lowest floor
(including basement) of all buildings, subject to §
154.11;
   (E)   Maintain a record of the engineer’s certificate and the as-built
floodproofed elevation of all buildings, subject to §
154.11(B)(4);
   (F)   Inspect all development projects to ensure they comply with the
provisions of this chapter;
   (G)   Cooperate with state and federal floodplain management agencies to
improve base flood and floodway data and to improve the administration of this
chapter. Submit reports as required for the National Flood Insurance Program;
and
   (H)   Maintain for public inspection and furnish upon request base flood
data, SFHA maps, copies of federal or state permit documents and as-built
elevation, and floodproofing data for all buildings constructed subject to this
chapter.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-3) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.04 BASE FLOOD ELEVATIONS.
   This chapter’s protection standard is the base flood. The best available
base flood data are listed below. Whenever a party disagrees with the best
available data, the party may finance the detailed engineering study needed to
replace existing data with better data and submit it to the State Water Survey
and the Federal Emergency Management Agency.
   (A)   The base flood elevation for each of the SFHAs delineated as an
unnumbered “A Zone” on the Flood Insurance Rate Map of the village shall be
according to the best data available to the State Water Survey Floodplain
Information Repository. When no base flood elevation exists, the base flood
elevation shall be the 100-year flood depth calculated according to the
formulas presented in “Depth and Frequency of Floods in Illinois” published by
the U.S. Geological Survey, 1976.
   (B)   The base flood elevation for the SFHAs of those parts of the
unincorporated county that are within the extraterritorial jurisdiction of the
village or that may be annexed into the village shall be according to the best
data available to the State Water Survey Floodplain Information Repository.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-4) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.05 PERMIT PROVISIONS.
   (A)   Development permit. No person not exempted by state law shall commence
any development in the SFHA without first obtaining a development permit from
the proper village official. The proper village official shall not issue a
development permit if the proposed development does not meet the requirements
of this chapter.
      (1)   Application and drawings. The application for a development permit
shall be accompanied by drawings of the site, drawn to scale, showing property
line dimensions, existing grade elevations, and all changes in grade resulting
from excavation or filling, the location and dimensions of all buildings and
additions to buildings, and the elevation of the lowest floor (including
basement) of all proposed buildings, subject to the requirements of §
154.11.
      (2)   Analyses of base flood elevation data. Upon receipt of an
application for a development permit, the proper village official shall compare
the elevation of the site to the base flood elevation. Any development located
on land that can be shown to have been higher than the base flood elevation as
of June 18, 1987, is not located in the SFHA and, therefore, not subject to the
requirements of this chapter. The proper village official shall maintain
documentation of the existing ground elevation at the development site and
certification that this ground elevation existed prior to June 18, 1987.
      (3)   Compliance with federal and state provisions required. The proper
village official shall inform the applicant of any and all other local, state,
and federal permits that may be required for this type of development activity.
The proper village official shall not issue the development permit unless all
required federal and state permits have been obtained.
      (4)   Conditions of permit; expiration.
         (a)   All work performed under a development permit shall conform to
the approved application and plans, and approved amendments thereto.
         (b)   A copy of the development permit shall be posted in a
conspicuous place on the premises, in plain view from a public road, during the
execution of the work and until completion of the same.
         (c)   A development permit under which no work is commenced within six
months after issuance shall expire by limitation and a new development permit
shall be secured before work is started.
   (B)   Use permits. It shall be unlawful to use or occupy any buildings or
any development site or any part thereof hereafter constructed in the SFHA
without first making application for obtaining a use permit from the proper
village official. Request for a use permit shall be submitted to the proper
village official upon completion of the work authorized in the development
permit issued for the project in question. If, upon final inspection of a
building or development site, the proper village official finds that the work
has been performed in accordance with the approved application and plans of the
development permit, a use permit may be issued. If final inspection reveals
otherwise, a use permit may not be issued until all deficiencies are corrected.
   (C)   Review of applications for permits; issuance or rejection. The proper
village official shall examine each application for a permit within three days’
time after filing. If, after examination, it appears that the proposed
development or use of the site will be in compliance with this chapter and such
other ordinances and resolutions passed by the Board of Trustees applicable
thereto, the proper village official shall approve such application and issue
the appropriate permit as soon as practicable. If the examination reveals
otherwise, the application shall be rejected and the proper village official’s
findings shall be noted in a written report to be attached to the application.
A copy of the report shall be provided to the applicant upon request.
   (D)   Revocation of permit.
      (1)   The proper village official may revoke a permit in case there has
been any false statement or misrepresentation as to a material fact in the
application or plans on which the permit was based.
      (2)   The proper village official shall inform the permittee, in writing,
of the specific steps the permittee must take in order to have the permit
reissued. It shall be unlawful to continue any work authorized by a permit
after revocation of that permit and until the permit is reissued or until a new
permit is issued.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-5) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987) Penalty, see §
154.99
§ 154.06 PERMIT FEES.
   Fees for permits shall be as follows.
   (A)   For a use permit: No fee.
   (B)   For a development permit for clearing debris, demolishing buildings,
or removing buildings out of the SFHA: No fee.
   (C)   For construction of a building valued at more than $100,000: $100.
   (D)   For construction or reconstruction of a building valued at less than
$100,000 and for any other development project that requires site inspections
by the proper village official: $75.
   (E)   For improvements made to an existing building, for installing a
manufactured home on a permanent site, and for any other development project:
$50.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-6) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.07 INSPECTION OF WORK; INTERFERENCE WITH OFFICIAL PROHIBITED.
   All work for which a permit is required shall be subject to inspection by
the proper village official or duly authorized representative. It shall be
unlawful to refuse to permit the proper village official or the representative
to enter such premises or structure at any reasonable time to make an
inspection. It shall be unlawful to interfere with or hinder the proper village
official or the representative when in the performance of his or her duties.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-7) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987) Penalty, see §
154.99
§ 154.08 COMPLETION OF WORK HERETOFORE AUTHORIZED.
   Nothing in this chapter shall require changes in the plans, construction, or
designated use of a building or other development project, or portion thereof,
which has been otherwise lawfully authorized, or the construction of which
shall have been actually begun and which entire building or development project
shall be completed within one year of the effective date hereof.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-8) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.09 FLOOD HAZARD DEVELOPMENT REQUIREMENTS.
   The Board of Trustees shall take into account flood hazards, to the extent
that they are known, in all official actions related to land management, use,
and development.
   (A)   New subdivisions, manufactured home parks, travel trailer parks,
annexation agreements, planned unit developments (PUDs), and additions to
manufactured home parks and subdivisions shall meet the requirements of §§
154.10 and
154.11. Plats or plans for new subdivisions, manufactured home parks, and
planned unit developments (PUDs) shall include a signed statement by a
registered professional engineer that the plat or plan accounts for changes in
the drainage of surface waters in accordance with the Plat Act (765 ILCS 205/
1 et seq.).
   (B)   (1)   Proposals for new subdivisions, manufactured home parks, travel
trailer parks, planned unit developments (PUDs), and additions to manufactured
home parks and subdivisions shall include base flood elevation data and
floodway delineations.
      (2)   Where this information is not available from an existing study
filed with the State Water Survey, the applicant shall be responsible for
calculating the base flood elevation and the floodway delineation and
submitting it to the State Water Survey for review and approval as best
available regulatory data.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-9) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.10 PREVENTING INCREASED DAMAGES.
   No development in the SFHA shall create a damaging or potentially damaging
increase in flood heights or velocity or threat to public health and safety.
   (A)   Riverine SFHAs. Within all riverine SFHAs, the following standards
shall apply,
      (1)   In addition to the other requirements of this chapter, a
development permit for a site located in a floodway (or in a riverine SFHA
where no floodway has been identified) shall not be issued unless the applicant
first obtains a permit or written documentation that a permit is not required
from the State Department of Transportation, Division of Water Resources,
issued pursuant to 615 ILCS 5/5 et seq.
      (2)   The following activities may be constructed without the individual
permit required in division (A)(1) above in accordance with statewide permits
issued by the State Department of Transportation, Division of Water Resources;
provided, the activities do not involve placement of fill, change of grade or
construction in the normal channel. Such activities must still meet the other
requirements of this chapter:
         (a)   The construction of wells, septic tanks, and underground utility
lines not crossing a lake or stream;
         (b)   The construction of light poles, sign posts, and similar
structures;
         (c)   The construction of sidewalks, driveways, athletic fields
(excluding fences), patios, and similar surfaces which are built at grade;
         (d)   The construction of properly anchored, unwalled, open structures
such as playground equipment, pavilions, and carports;
         (e)   The placement of properly anchored buildings not exceeding 70
square feet in size, nor ten feet in any dimension (for example, animal
shelters and tool sheds); and
         (f)   The construction of additions to existing buildings which do not
increase the first floor area by more than 20%, which are located on the
upstream or downstream side of the existing building and which do not extend
beyond the sides of the existing building that are parallel to the flow of
flood water.
      (3)   The total cumulative effect of the proposed development, when
combined with all other existing and anticipated development, will not increase
the base flood elevation more than one foot for the affected hydraulic reach of
the stream and will not increase flood damage or potential flood damage.
   (B)   Public health standards in all SFHAs.
      (1)   No development in the SFHA shall include locating or storing
chemicals, explosives, buoyant materials, flammable liquids, pollutants, or
other hazardous or toxic materials below the FPE unless such materials are
stored in a storage tank or floodproofed building constructed according to the
requirements of §
154.11(B)(4).
      (2)   New and replacement sanitary sewer lines and on-site waste disposal
systems may be permitted, providing all manholes or other above-ground openings
located below the FPE are watertight.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-10) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987) Penalty, see §
154.99
§ 154.11 PROTECTION OF BUILDINGS.
   (A)   Protection required. In addition to the damage prevention requirements
of §
154.10, all buildings to be located in the SFHA shall be protected from flood
damage below the FPE. This building protection requirement applies to the
following situations:
      (1)   Construction or placement of a new building valued at more than
$1,000;
      (2)   Structural alterations made to an existing building that increase
the floor area by more than 20% or the market value of the building by more
than 50%;
      (3)   Reconstruction or repairs made to a damaged building that are
valued at more than 50% of the markd value of the building before the damage
occurred;
      (4)   Installing a manufactured home on a new site or a new manufactured
home on an existing site. This chapter does not apply to returning a
manufactured home to the same site it lawfully occupied before it was removed
to avoid flood damage; and
      (5)   Installing a travel trailer on a site for more than 180 days.
   (B)   Methods of protection. This building protection requirement may be met
by one of the following methods. The proper village official shall maintain a
record of compliance with these building protection standards as required in §
154.03.
      (1)   A residential or non-residential building may be constructed on
permanent land fill in accordance with the following:
         (a)   The fill shall be placed in layers no greater than one foot deep
before compaction;
         (b)   The lowest floor (including basement) shall be at or above the
FPE. The fill should extend at least ten feet beyond the foundation of the
building before sloping below the FPE;
         (c)   The fill shall be protected against erosion and scour during the
flooding by vegetative cover, rip rap, or bulkheading. If vegetative cover is
used, the slopes shall be no steeper than three horizontal to one vertical; and
         (d)   The fill shall not adversely affect the flow of surface drainage
from or onto neighboring properties.
      (2)   A residential or non-residential building may be elevated in
accordance with the following:
         (a)   The building or improvements shall be elevated on crawl space,
walls, stilts, piles, or other foundation, provided:
            1.   The walls have permanent openings no more than one foot above
grade; and
            2.   The walls and floor are not subject to damage by hydrostatic
pressures associated with the base flood.
         (b)   The foundation and supporting members shall be anchored and
aligned in relation to flood flows and adjacent structures so as to minimize
exposure to known hydrodynamic forces such as currents, waves, ice, and
floating debris; and
         (c)   All areas below the FPE shall be constructed of materials
resistant to flood damage. The lowest floor (including basement) and all
electrical, heating, ventilating, plumbing, and air conditioning equipment and
utility meters shall be located at or above the FPE. Water and sewer pipes,
electrical and telephone lines, submersible pumps, and other waterproofed
service facilities may be located below the FPE.
      (3)   Manufactured homes and travel trailers to be installed on a site
for more than 180 days shall be:
         (a)   Elevated at or above the FPE in accordance with divisions (B)(1)
or (B)(2) above; and
         (b)   Anchored to resist flotation, collapse, or lateral movement by
being tied down in accordance with the rules and regulations for the State
Mobile Home Tie-Down Act issued pursuant to 210 ILCS 120/1 et seq.
      (4)   Only a non-residential building may be floodproofed in accordance
with the following.
         (a)   A registered professional engineer shall certify that the
building has been designed so that below the FPE, and the structure and
attendant utility facilities are watertight and capable of resisting the
effects of the base flood. The building design shall take into account flood
velocities, duration, rate of rise, hydrostatic and hydrodynamic forces, the
effects of buoyancy, and impacts from debris or ice.
         (b)   Floodproofing measures shall be operable without human
intervention and without an outside source of electricity.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-11) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.12 VARIANCES.
   Whenever the standards of this chapter place undue hardship on a specific
development proposal, the applicant may apply to the village for a variance.
The village shall review the applicant’s request for a variance and shall
submit its recommendation to the Board of Trustees. The Board of Trustees may
attach such conditions to granting of a variance as it deems necessary to
further the intent of this chapter.
   (A)   Criteria for variance. No variance shall be granted unless the
applicant demonstrates that:
      (1)   The development activity cannot be located outside the SFHA;
      (2)   An exceptional hardship would result if the variance were not
granted;
      (3)   The relief requested is the minimum necessary;
      (4)   There will be no additional threat to public health or safety or
creation of a nuisance;
      (5)   There will be no additional public expense for flood protection,
rescue or relief operations, policing or repairs to roads, utilities, or other
public facilities;
      (6)   The provisions of §
154.05(A)(3) are met; and
      (7)   The provisions of §
154.10(A) are met.
   (B)   Notification to applicant of consequences. The village shall notify an
applicant, in writing, that a variance from the requirements of §
154.11 that would lessen the degree of protection to a building will:
      (1)   Result in increased premium rates for flood insurance up to amounts
that may be as high as $25 for $100 of insurance coverage;
      (2)   Increase the risks to life and property; and
      (3)   Require that the applicant proceed with knowledge of these risks
and that the applicant acknowledge, in writing, the assumption of the risk and
liability.
   (C)   Historic places. Variances to the building protection requirements of
§
154.11 requested in connection with the reconstruction, repair, or alteration
of a site or building included on the National Register of Historic Places or
the State Register of Historic Places may be granted using criteria more
permissive than the requirements of divisions (A)(1) through (A)(5) above.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-12) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.13 DISCLAIMER OF LIABILITY.
   (A)   The degree of flood protection required by this chapter is considered
reasonable for regulatory purposes and is based on available information
derived from engineering and scientific methods of study. Larger floods may
occur or flood heights may be increased by human-made or natural causes.
   (B)   This chapter does not imply that development either inside or outside
of the SFHA will be free from flooding or damage.
   (C)   This chapter does not create liability on the part of the village or
any officer or employee thereof for any flood damage that results from reliance
on this chapter or any administrative decision lawfully thereunder.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-13) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.14 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   Violations; notice.
      (1)   Failure to obtain a permit for development in the SFHA or failure
to comply with the requirements of a permit or conditions of a variance
resolution shall be deemed to be a violation of this chapter.
      (2)   Upon due investigation, the Village Attorney may determine that a
violation of the minimum standards of this chapter exists. The Village Attorney
shall notify the owner, in writing, of such violation.
   (B)   Suspension of insurance. The Village Attorney shall inform the owner
that any such violation is considered a willful act to increase flood damages
and, therefore, may cause coverage by a standard flood insurance policy to be
suspended.
   (C)   Additional remedies; costs. Nothing herein shall prevent the village
from taking such other lawful action to prevent or remedy any violations. All
costs connected therewith shall accrue to the person responsible.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-14) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.15 ABROGATION AND GREATER RESTRICTIONS.
   This chapter repeals and replaces other ordinances adopted by the Board of
Trustees to fulfill the requirements of the National Flood Insurance Program.
However, this chapter does not repeal the original resolution or ordinance
adopted to achieve eligibility in the Program. Nor does this chapter repeal,
abrogate, or impair any existing easements, covenants, or deed restrictions.
Where this chapter and other ordinance easements, covenants, or deed
restrictions conflict or overlap, whichever imposes the more stringent
restrictions shall prevail.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-15) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
§ 154.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   If such owner of a permit fails, after ten days’ notice, to correct
the violation, as stated in §
154.14:
      (1)   The village may make application to the Circuit Court for an
injunction requiring conformance with this chapter or make such other order as
the court deems necessary to secure compliance with this chapter;
      (2)   Any person who violates this chapter shall, upon conviction
thereof, be fined not less than $25 nor more than $200; and
      (3)   A separate offense shall be deemed committed upon each day during
or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, § 9-1-14) (Ord. passed 5-5-1987)
